# Annotated bibliography

[Reference contents](../index.md) · [Conclusion register](claims.md) · [Structured register](references.json) · [Search history](searches.md)

Checked **2026-10-02**. This register includes research, consensus, commentary and a small number of definition/documentation sources; it is not a count of independent trials. Duplicate DOI/PMID records are merged, while citation aliases and per-chapter extractions remain traceable in JSON. Access labels distinguish full text, abstract and metadata; a source may have different access/extraction detail across chapters. No total participant count is implied.

## Read first

| Paper / source record | Why it is useful; main boundary |
|---|---|
| [Joyner MJ, Coyle EF (2008): Endurance exercise performance: the physiology of champions](#phy01) | Narrative review. Champion endurance athletes; synthesis, not a new cohort. Review, not a training trial. Does not prescribe amateur hours. |
| [Jones AM, Carter H (2000): The effect of endurance training on parameters of aerobic fitness](#phy09) | Narrative review. Endurance athletes. Review from 2000. Does not give amateur hour prescriptions. |
| [Poole DC, Rossiter HB, Brooks GA, Gladden LB (2021): The anaerobic threshold: 50+ years of controversy](#phy13) | Narrative review. Exercise physiology. Conceptual review. Does not validate a zone chart. |
| [Molmen KS, Almquist NW, Skattebo O (2025): Effects of exercise training on mitochondrial and capillary growth in human skeletal muscle: a systematic review and meta-regression](#phy23) | Systematic review and meta-regression. 5973 participants; 353 mitochondrial and 131 capillary articles. Well-trained mean VO2max 62.2±6.7, few groups, 97% men. ET bin is everything below VT2, not Zone 2. Mostly untrained. Not cycling performance. No newer meta-analysis found through 2026-10-02. |
| [Cove B, Chalmers S, Nelson MJ, Anderson M, Bennett H (2025): The effect of training distribution, duration, and volume on VO2max and performance in trained cyclists: a systematic review, multilevel meta-analysis, and multivariate meta-regression](#phy29) | Multilevel meta-analysis and meta-regression. Trained cyclists. Abstract: 41 studies, 81 groups, 797 participants. Inclusion was sex-specific Tier 3 study-mean VO2max (men >=59, women >=54.4 mL/kg/min), not every participant >=59.. POL meant LIT or habitual training plus intervals shorter than 5 min above MLSS or at the highest sustainable intensity, not a measured 80/20 or below-LT1 distribution. Intervals longer than 5 min were non-polarized. Counts are internally inconsistent (abstract 41 studies; discussion 44; 23 randomized plus 21 non-randomized). Do not use body confidence intervals. Do not copy the reported weekly volume 4.9 plus or minus 7.3 h (range 2.6-14.3): that SD is implausible for the range. Certainty low. Null volume regression is not a causal optimum or proof that extra hours cannot help. |
| [Moran-MacDonald A, Wilson S, Wu Z, Haines G, Beaupre L, Aube J, Andrews C, Jonsson N, Gurd BJ (2026): The effectiveness of polarized versus high-intensity training in moderately trained cyclists: a randomized parallel-design study](#phy61) | Randomized parallel added-volume trial. 19 moderately trained cyclists/triathletes, 17 men; baseline VO2max not reported in the retrieved abstract. Small, short, mostly male and moderately trained; total load not matched. Nonsignificance is not equivalence or a long-term dose optimum. Does not test a 6/8/10/12 h dose curve. The abstract's internally questionable exploratory eta-squared/power statement is not interpreted. |
| [Rosenblat MA et al. (2025): Which training intensity distribution intervention will produce the greatest improvements in maximal oxygen uptake and time-trial performance in endurance athletes? A systematic review and network meta-analysis of individual participant data](#tid07) | IPD network meta-analysis. 348 endurance athletes (296 male, 52 female); 150 recreational, 198 competitive. Abstract only; not cyclist-only; nonsignificance is not equivalence. |
| [Seiler S, Jøranson K, Olesen BV, Hetlelid KJ (2013): Adaptations to aerobic interval training: interactive effects of exercise intensity and total work duration](#tid48) | 7-week randomized comparison of exclusive interval durations. 35 recreational cyclists, 29 men 6 women, VO2peak 52±6, ~6 h/week. Abstract only. Recreational, not well-trained. n=9 per interval arm. |
| [Rønnestad BR, Hansen J, Vegge G, Tønnessen E, Slettaløkken G (2015): Short intervals induce superior training adaptations compared with long intervals in cyclists - an effort-matched approach](#int03) | Randomized 10-week training comparison, effort-matched not power-matched. Trained-to-well-trained male competitive cyclists; 20 randomized, 9 short and 7 long completers. Male only. Small n. Dropouts. Effort matching confounds duration with power. Mechanism not measured in this trial. |
| [Rønnestad BR, Hansen J, Nygaard H, Lundby C (2020): Superior performance improvements in elite cyclists following short-interval vs effort-matched long-interval training](#int04) | 3-week randomized comparison, effort-matched by RPE. 18 elite cyclists, VO2max 73±4 mL/kg/min, n=9 per group. Abstract only. Very short. Elite male-dominated sample. One lab. |
| [Sylta Ø et al. (2016): The effect of different high-intensity periodization models on endurance adaptations](#tid17) | Randomized 3-group, load-matched. 63 cyclists, VO2peak 61.3±5.8. Candidate for the prompt's sequencing claim; transcript not supplied. Abstract only. Do not map increasing to VO2-first or decreasing to threshold-first. |
| [Odden I, Nymoen L, Urianstad T, Kristoffersen M, Hammarström D, Hansen J, Mølmen KS, Rønnestad BR (2024): The higher the fraction of maximal oxygen uptake is during interval training, the greater is the cycling performance gain](#int01) | 9-week training intervention; %VO2max measured every session; regression plus post-hoc median split, not randomized to high vs low fraction. 22 well-trained cyclists, 19 men 3 women, VO2max 67.1±6.4 mL/kg/min. Association not causation. Post-hoc split. Easy-volume imbalance. Three women. R2adj is not fraction-alone variance. Title overclaims. |
| [Bossi AH, Mesquida C, Passfield L, Rønnestad BR, Hopker JG (2020): Optimizing interval training through power-output variation within the work intervals](#int07) | Acute crossover, matched duration and mean power. 14 cyclists, VO2max 69.2±6.6. Acute only. No adaptation follow-up. |
| [Hanstock HG, Govus AD, Stenqvist TB, Melin AK, Sylta Ø, Torstveit MK (2020): Influence of immune and nutritional biomarkers on illness risk during interval training](#int53) | Randomized 4-week comparison of three HIT prescriptions; primary outcomes were illness and biomarkers, not performance. 27 well-trained male cyclists and triathletes enrolled; 25 analyzed, age 29.9±9.1, VO2peak 64.0±6.4 mL/kg/min, performance level 3-4. Illness was the primary question. Small cells, n per arm not stated in text. Male only. Accepted manuscript, not the copyedited journal version. Does not show equivalence of formats. |
| [Rønnestad BR, Hansen J (2016): Optimizing interval training at power output associated with peak oxygen uptake in well-trained cyclists](#int54) | Acute randomized-order crossover to exhaustion; not a training study. 13 well-trained male cyclists, VO2peak 66.7±6.1 mL/kg/min, at least 6 h/week. Acute only. To exhaustion, not a fixed session. Male only. Journal issue 2016; DOI registered 2013. Stroke volume subset n=6. |
| [Sánchez-Jiménez JL, Salas-Montoro JA, Mateo-March M, Priego-Quesada JI, Zabala M, Pérez-Díaz JJ (2025): Is intensity the most important factor in determining the amount of prior work accumulated that affects cyclists' acute durability? A systematic review](#dur40) | Systematic review, 21 studies, search 18 Sep 2024. Cyclists, mixed level. Protocol heterogeneity; about 30% of studies found by hand search; does not include 2025-2026 trials after the search date. |
| [Norte BR, Slinn MM, Johnson KO, Mahon E, Shepherd SO, Strauss JA, Louis JB (2026): Graded Carbohydrate Ingestion up to 120 g·h−1 Attenuates the Reduction in Critical Power Following 3 h of Moderate-Intensity Exercise in a Dose-Dependent Manner](#dur51) | Crossover, 3 h at 95% GET with 0, 60 or 120 g/h CHO, 3-min CP estimate. 16 endurance-trained cyclists/triathletes, VO2max 51.8±6.8. 3-min estimate of CP; modest VO2max; n=16; 90 g/h not tested. Section heading says GI discomfort was not altered, but post hoc tests show 120 g/h worse than water on cumulative score and three peak symptoms. Not universal tolerance or intolerance. GET preload is not an FTP ride. |
| [Gejl KD, Nybo L (2021): Performance effects of periodized carbohydrate restriction in endurance trained athletes: systematic review and meta-analysis](#fuel36) | Systematic review and meta-analysis. 9 studies; VO2max ≥55 (women) or ≥60 (men) mL/kg/min. Nonsignificant pooled effect is imprecise, not proof of no benefit. Includes the positive Marquet trials. Heterogeneous protocols. |
| [Trommelen J, Fuchs CJ, Beelen M, Lenaerts K, Jeukendrup AE, Cermak NM, van Loon LJ (2017): Fructose and sucrose intake increase exogenous carbohydrate oxidation during exercise](#fuel16) | Crossover, stable isotope. 10 trained male cyclists, VO2peak 65 ± 2. Not a sucrose-alone versus free glucose-plus-fructose comparison. Moderate intensity. Men only. Open access PMC5331598. |
| [Gonzalez JT, Fuchs CJ, Smith FE, et al. (2015): Ingestion of glucose or sucrose prevents liver but not muscle glycogen depletion during prolonged exercise in trained cyclists](#fuel20) | Crossover with 13C-MRS. 14 cyclists; 4 also did water. Abstract-only. Moderate intensity. Water arm n=4. |
| [Wilson PB (2026): A narrative review of the high-carbohydrate fueling revolution (≥100 g/h) in the professional peloton](#fuel05) | Narrative review; open full text read for key points and ACSM quotation. Professional cycling context; synthesises laboratory trials. Narrative review. Multi-day benefit is theoretical/observational, not an RCT. |
| [Fuchs CJ, Veeraiah P, Hermans WJH, et al. (2025): Carbohydrate intake of 10 g/kg rapidly replenishes liver, but not muscle glycogen, during 12 h of recovery in well-trained cyclists](#fuel52) | Crossover with 13C-MRS and biopsies. 12 well-trained male cyclists, VO2peak 67 ± 5, Wmax 5.8 ± 0.7 W/kg. Abstract-only. Men. Severe depletion model. |
| [Roberts SSH et al. (2019): Extended Sleep Maintains Endurance Performance Better than Normal or Restricted Sleep](#rec05) | Randomised crossover. 9 endurance cyclists/triathletes. n=9. Acute repeated TT, not training adaptation. Abstract-only. |
| [Walsh NP et al. (2021): Sleep and the athlete: narrative review and 2021 expert consensus recommendations](#rec01) | Narrative review and expert consensus. Elite athletes; consensus panel. Few female experimental data; not a meta-analysis of cycling. |
| [McDonald P et al. (2025): Influence of Exercise Heat Acclimation Protocol Characteristics on Adaptation Kinetics](#rec29) | Bayesian meta-regression. 211 papers. Heat-performance outcomes. Hb-mass slope is not a daily prescription. |
| [Sim M et al. (2019): Iron considerations for the athlete: a narrative review](#rec59) | Narrative review. Athlete cohorts. Prevalence depends on cut-off and cohort. |
| [Llanos-Lagos C, Ramirez-Campillo R, Saez de Villarreal E (2026): Heavy strength training effects on physiological determinants of endurance cyclist performance: a systematic review with meta-analysis](#str01) | Systematic review with meta-analysis of controlled trials. 262 endurance cyclists (60 women); mean baseline VO2max 61.25 ml/kg/min. Low certainty from risk of bias and imprecision; some non-random allocation; no significant moderators so dose is not optimised. Efficiency text point +0.353 with CI -0.619 to -0.088 conflicts with the negative Figure 3 pool; do not treat that pair as one estimate. |
| [Quittmann OJ; Piehl MA (2026): The 3-min all-out test in cycling: a systematic review and meta-analysis on its reliability and validity](#mon37) | Systematic review and meta-analysis. 20 studies, N=284, mostly small and male, 2006-2025. Full text. Heterogeneous protocols. Prediction intervals wide. |
| [Saw AE; Main LC; Gastin PB (2016): Monitoring the athlete training response: subjective self-reported measures trump commonly used objective measures](#mon39) | Systematic review. 54 studies of athletes in training. Full text consulted. Not cycling-specific. Many small studies. |

## Full source annotations

Primary group headings organize retrieval, not exclusive scientific ownership. Shared sources link to every relevant chapter.

## Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity

<a id="phy01"></a>
### PHY01 — Endurance exercise performance: the physiology of champions

**Joyner MJ, Coyle EF; 2008.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1113/jphysiol.2007.143834). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1113/jphysiol.2007.143834`. PMID: [17901124](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17901124/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Champion endurance athletes; synthesis, not a new cohort.
- **Verified extraction:** Performance VO2 is VO2max times sustainable fraction; efficiency converts that VO2 into speed or power. Genetic and motivational factors also decide who becomes a champion.
- **Limitations:** Review, not a training trial. Does not prescribe amateur hours.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=DOI:10.1113/jphysiol.2007.143834&resultType=core)

<a id="phy02"></a>
### PHY02 — Limiting factors for maximum oxygen uptake and determinants of endurance performance

**Bassett DR, Howley ET; 2000.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1097/00005768-200001000-00012). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1097/00005768-200001000-00012`. PMID: [10647532](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10647532/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Exercising humans; endurance performance synthesis.
- **Verified extraction:** VO2max limited primarily by oxygen delivery. Mitochondrial adaptations improve submaximal endurance by raising fat oxidation and lowering lactate at a given VO2. LT speed integrates VO2max, economy and fractional utilization.
- **Limitations:** Review. Training evidence for a-vO2 difference cited is older than Montero 2015.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy03"></a>
### PHY03 — Factors limiting maximal performance in humans

**di Prampero PE; 2003.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-003-0926-z). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-003-0926-z`. PMID: [12910345](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12910345/).
- **Design / duration:** Theoretical model; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Model of track running; O2 conductance equation.
- **Verified extraction:** For two-leg exercise, circulatory O2 transport estimated as about 75% of post-lung resistance to VO2max. Mitochondrial capacity and diffusion share the rest.
- **Limitations:** Model, not a cyclist intervention. Percentages are theoretical.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy04"></a>
### PHY04 — Biology of VO2max: looking under the physiology lamp

**Lundby C, Montero D, Joyner M; 2017.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/apha.12827). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/apha.12827`. PMID: [27888580](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27888580/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Training and athlete cross-sections.
- **Verified extraction:** Training-induced VO2max increases are largely via red-cell volume expansion and stroke volume. Previously untrained people respond if dose is sufficient.
- **Limitations:** Review. Does not quantify 7-12 h amateur gains.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy05"></a>
### PHY05 — Endurance training and VO2max: role of maximal cardiac output and oxygen extraction

**Montero D, Diaz-Canestro C, Lundby C; 2015.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0000000000000640). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/mss.0000000000000640`. PMID: [25680086](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25680086/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and data pooling; 5 to 12.9 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 13 studies, 130 untrained or moderately trained healthy young adults, mean age 22-28.
- **Verified extraction:** VO2max SMD 0.75. Conclusion in retrieved abstract: improvement associated with increase in maximal cardiac output, not arteriovenous O2 difference. Exact Qmax SMD truncated in first Europe PMC pull; conclusion verified.
- **Limitations:** Not well-trained cyclists. Small evidence base. Some effect sizes not fully captured in the retrieved abstract fragment.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy06"></a>
### PHY06 — Haematological rather than skeletal muscle adaptations contribute to the increase in peak oxygen uptake induced by moderate endurance training

**Montero D, Cathomen A, Jacobs RA, et al.; 2015.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1113/jp270250). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1113/jp270250`. PMID: [26282186](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26282186/).
- **Design / duration:** Controlled training plus phlebotomy; 6 weeks, 3-4 sessions/week.
- **Population / baseline:** 16 untrained healthy men, age 25±4, VO2peak 3.5±0.5 L/min.
- **Verified extraction:** VO2peak +9±8%, Qpeak +7±6%, RBCV +8±4%, plasma volume +4±6%, Hb mass +6±5%, capillary-to-fibre +18±16%, mitochondrial volume density +43±30%. Phlebotomy returning RBCV to baseline reduced VO2peak -7±7% and Qpeak -10±7%. OXPHOS unchanged.
- **Limitations:** Untrained men. Moderate endurance training, not a cyclist TID comparison.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy07"></a>
### PHY07 — Determinants of maximal oxygen uptake in highly trained females and males

**Skattebo O, Martin-Rincon M, Rud B, et al.; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1113/jp289218). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1113/jp289218`. PMID: [40974561](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40974561/).
- **Design / duration:** Cross-sectional invasive mechanistic study; acute maximal test.
- **Population / baseline:** 23 highly trained cyclists and triathletes, 10 women, age 29±6.
- **Verified extraction:** Maximal cardiac output 22±3 vs 30±3 L/min. Leg O2 extraction 91±3% vs 92±3%, similar mitochondria and capillaries. Women had ~10% lower Hb concentration and lower LBM-normalised O2 delivery and VO2max.
- **Limitations:** Cross-sectional, not a training intervention. Small female n.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy08"></a>
### PHY08 — A review of central and peripheral limitations to endurance exercise performance on the road to the Milano-Cortina 2026 Olympics

**Skattebo O, Martin-Rincon M, Calbet JAL, Hallen J, Holmberg HC; 2026.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.70266). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/sms.70266`. PMID: [41845944](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41845944/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Elite endurance athletes, winter-sport emphasis.
- **Verified extraction:** Systemic O2 delivery is the primary limiter of VO2max in elite athletes. Mitochondrial density and capillarisation support fractional utilization and extraction, which may exceed 95% in some elite skiers. Most fractional-utilization values in the literature are estimates.
- **Limitations:** First publication 2026-03-01, before cutoff. Scarce invasive female data. Not a cycling dose trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy09"></a>
### PHY09 — The effect of endurance training on parameters of aerobic fitness

**Jones AM, Carter H; 2000.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.2165/00007256-200029060-00001). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.2165/00007256-200029060-00001`. PMID: [10870864](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10870864/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Endurance athletes.
- **Verified extraction:** Key aerobic parameters: VO2max, economy, lactate/ventilatory threshold, VO2 kinetics; related markers include velocity at VO2max and MLSS or critical power.
- **Limitations:** Review from 2000. Does not give amateur hour prescriptions.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy10"></a>
<a id="dur08"></a>
### PHY10 — The maximal metabolic steady state: redefining the gold standard

**Jones AM, Burnley M, Black MI, Poole DC, Vanhatalo A; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.14814/phy2.14098). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.14814/phy2.14098`. PMID: [31124324](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31124324/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Exercise physiology synthesis.
- **Verified extraction:** MLSS as commonly measured underestimates the maximal metabolic steady state. CP is the heavy-severe boundary. Exercise at CP is typically sustainable for about 20-30 min.
- **Limitations:** Argumentative review. Sustainability range is typical, not a universal constant.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC6533178), [source 2](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC6533178/fullTextXML)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY10`, `DUR08`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy11"></a>
<a id="dur07"></a>
### PHY11 — Relative proximity of critical power and metabolic/ventilatory thresholds: systematic review and meta-analysis

**Galan-Rioja MA, Gonzalez-Mohino F, Poole DC, Gonzalez-Rave JM; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-020-01314-8). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-020-01314-8`. PMID: [32613479](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32613479/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; n/a cross-sectional comparisons.
- **Population / baseline:** 9 studies, 104 participants.
- **Verified extraction:** Pooled r=0.73 between CP and thresholds. VT1 underestimated CP by ~30%, MLSS by ~11%, RCP overestimated by ~6%, VT2 by ~21%. CP and VT2 correlation not significant in subgroup.
- **Limitations:** Small study set. Inclusion wording required RCTs, which is an odd filter for correlation studies. Offsets are not individual constants.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/32613479)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY11`, `DUR07`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy12"></a>
### PHY12 — Power-duration relationship: physiology, fatigue, and the limits of human performance

**Burnley M, Jones AM; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1080/17461391.2016.1249524). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1080/17461391.2016.1249524`. PMID: [27806677](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27806677/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Human power-duration physiology.
- **Verified extraction:** Only a small power range is sustainable, below CP. Moderate domain fatigue is slow and largely central; heavy domain involves glycogen-related fatigue; severe domain loses metabolic homeostasis and VO2 rises toward max. W-prime is finite work above CP.
- **Limitations:** Review. W-prime is model-dependent.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy13"></a>
### PHY13 — The anaerobic threshold: 50+ years of controversy

**Poole DC, Rossiter HB, Brooks GA, Gladden LB; 2021.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1113/jp279963). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1113/jp279963`. PMID: [33112439](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33112439/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Exercise physiology.
- **Verified extraction:** Lactate rise is not evidence of muscle anoxia. The threshold for a sustained non-oxidative contribution is critical power, often far above the lactate threshold.
- **Limitations:** Conceptual review. Does not validate a zone chart.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy14"></a>
### PHY14 — Physiological determinants of endurance exercise performance

**Coyle EF; 1999.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1016/s1440-2440%2899%2980172-8). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1016/S1440-2440(99)80172-8`. PMID: [10668757](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10668757/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review / model; events of 30 min to 4 h.
- **Population / baseline:** Endurance athletes.
- **Verified extraction:** Endurance model centres on lactate-threshold velocity and gross mechanical efficiency. At 80 rpm, efficiency positively related to type I fibre composition. Oxygen and blood-glucose availability also influence performance.
- **Limitations:** 1999 synthesis. Carbohydrate 30-60 g/h is historical relative to current fueling evidence and is not used as a prescription here.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy15"></a>
### PHY15 — The effects of training on gross efficiency in cycling: a review

**Hopker J, Passfield L, Coleman D, Jobson S, Edwards L, Carter H; 2009.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1055/s-0029-1237712). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1055/s-0029-1237712`. PMID: [19941249](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19941249/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Cyclists, cross-sectional and training studies.
- **Verified extraction:** Authors conclude well-controlled studies show training can raise gross efficiency; many null cross-sectional comparisons were methodologically weak. Mechanisms remain undetermined.
- **Limitations:** Review. Specific effect sizes not in the retrieved abstract.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy16"></a>
### PHY16 — Inverse relationship between VO2max and gross efficiency

**Hopker J, Coleman D, Jobson SA, Passfield L; 2012.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1055/s-0032-1304640). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1055/s-0032-1304640`. PMID: [22562732](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22562732/).
- **Design / duration:** Two training experiments; season and 12 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** Experiment 1: 14 trained cyclists across a season. Experiment 2: 29 trained cyclists, 12 weeks.
- **Verified extraction:** Training raised gross efficiency but not VO2max. Within-subject inverse correlation r=-0.32 across the season and r=-0.78 in the high-intensity group over the first 6 weeks.
- **Limitations:** Self-coached season in experiment 1. Not a randomized intensity-percentage trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy17"></a>
### PHY17 — Prolonged constant load cycling exercise is associated with reduced gross efficiency and increased muscle oxygen uptake

**Hopker JG, O'Grady C, Pageaux B; 2017.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.12673). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/sms.12673`. PMID: [26993076](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26993076/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute repeated-measures; 2 h at 60% of maximal minute power.
- **Population / baseline:** 14 well-trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Gross efficiency was tracked at 5, 30, 60, 90 and 120 min during prolonged constant-load cycling. Title and abstract report reduced gross efficiency with increased muscle oxygen uptake. Magnitude of the GE drop was truncated in the retrieved abstract and is not stated.
- **Limitations:** Acute, not a training effect. 60% of maximal minute power is not 60% FTP.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy18"></a>
### PHY18 — Determinants of time trial performance and maximal incremental exercise in highly trained endurance athletes

**Jacobs RA, Rasmussen P, Siebenmann C, et al.; 2011.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1152/japplphysiol.00625.2011). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1152/japplphysiol.00625.2011`. PMID: [21885805](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21885805/).
- **Design / duration:** Cross-sectional multivariate; 26 km time trial and incremental test.
- **Population / baseline:** 16 highly trained cyclists; 150 variables reduced to 10 factors.
- **Verified extraction:** Time-trial power associated with vastus lateralis oxidative phosphorylation capacity, submaximal lactate, and maximal leg oxygenation, accounting for 78% of variance. Maximal power associated with Hb mass, VO2max and leg oxygenation.
- **Limitations:** Small n, large predictor set, overfitting risk. Not a training trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy19"></a>
<a id="dur48"></a>
### PHY19 — The importance of durability in the physiological profiling of endurance athletes

**Maunder E, Seiler S, Mildenhall MJ, Kilding AE, Plews DJ; 2021.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-021-01459-0). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-021-01459-0`. PMID: [33886100](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33886100/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review with field illustration; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Endurance athletes.
- **Verified extraction:** Physiological profiling variables change during prolonged exercise. Durability defined as timing and magnitude of that deterioration. Fresh profiles mislead intensity regulation and performance prediction if durability is ignored.
- **Limitations:** Conceptual. Does not prove a training method.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/33886100)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY19`, `DUR48`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy20"></a>
<a id="dur42"></a>
### PHY20 — The relationship between training characteristics and durability in professional cyclists across a competitive season

**Spragg J, Leo P, Swart J; 2023.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1080/17461391.2022.2049886). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1080/17461391.2022.2049886`. PMID: [35239466](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35239466/).
- **Design / duration:** Observational season analysis; competitive season, early/mid/late.
- **Population / baseline:** 30 male U23 professional cyclists, VO2max 73.8±2.5, CP 5.48±0.38 W/kg.
- **Verified extraction:** Fatigued power profile (after 2000 kJ) varied more across the season than the fresh profile. Time below VT1 correlated with fatigued 5-min (r=0.43, p=0.018) and 12-min power (r=0.376, p=0.04). A shift toward polarized distribution correlated with fatigued 12-min power (r=0.414, p=0.023).
- **Limitations:** Observational, one level of rider, not a randomized volume trial. Abstract punctuation was messy; correlations cited are those explicitly recoverable.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/35239466)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY20`, `DUR42`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy21"></a>
### PHY21 — Power output at the moderate-to-heavy intensity transition decreases in a non-linear fashion during prolonged exercise

**Gallo G, Faelli EL, Ruggeri P, et al.; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-024-05440-3). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-024-05440-3`. PMID: [38483635](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38483635/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute prolonged cycling; to task failure, 234±66 min.
- **Population / baseline:** 12 trained cyclists, age 40±8, VO2peak 52.3±5.2.
- **Verified extraction:** VT1 power fell as a second-order polynomial in 11 of 12. Time to 5% VT1 drop 139±78 min correlated with time to failure (rs=0.676). Related to rested fat-oxidation rates.
- **Limitations:** Acute, not a training study. Recreationally trained rather than WorldTour.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy22"></a>
### PHY22 — Training characteristics and power profile of professional U23 cyclists throughout a competitive season

**Leo P, Spragg J, Simon D, Lawley JS, Mujika I; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.3390/sports8120167). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.3390/sports8120167`. PMID: [33348618](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33348618/).
- **Design / duration:** Observational season monitoring; one season in four calendar periods.
- **Population / baseline:** 30 male U23 professionals, age 20.0±1.0, VO2max 73.7±2.5.
- **Verified extraction:** Period hours: pre 167±46, early 202±28, mid 219±26, late 150±36. VT1 256±22 W, VT2 367±38 W, CP 382±33 W, W-prime 17.8±3.6 kJ. Season HR distribution about 17-19% below VT1, 51-63% between thresholds, 8-9% above VT2.
- **Limitations:** One team. Includes racing. Weekly hours in the brief are derived from calendar windows, not reported by the paper. Heart-rate zones can misclassify intensity.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC7766290)

<a id="phy23"></a>
### PHY23 — Effects of exercise training on mitochondrial and capillary growth in human skeletal muscle: a systematic review and meta-regression

**Molmen KS, Almquist NW, Skattebo O; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-024-02120-2). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-024-02120-2`. PMID: [39390310](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39390310/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-regression; search to 2 February 2022; interventions of varying weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 5973 participants; 353 mitochondrial and 131 capillary articles. Well-trained mean VO2max 62.2±6.7, few groups, 97% men.
- **Verified extraction:** Adjusted mitochondrial content +23±5% ET, +27±5% HIT, +27±7% SIT. Per hour SIT ~3.9x ET and HIT ~1.7x ET. Frequency 6>4>2 sessions/week. Well-trained pooled mito estimate +6.6±6.5% (P=0.046). Per-hour endurance-training estimate in that stratum was not statistically distinguished from zero (P=0.933); imprecise, not proof that extra easy hours cannot change mitochondria. Capillaries per fibre similar across modalities and mostly <4 weeks; not clear in well-trained. Load = volume x intensity.
- **Limitations:** ET bin is everything below VT2, not Zone 2. Mostly untrained. Not cycling performance. No newer meta-analysis found through 2026-10-02.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC11787188), [source 2](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39390310/)

<a id="phy24"></a>
### PHY24 — Training-induced changes in mitochondrial content and respiratory function in human skeletal muscle

**Granata C, Jamnick NA, Bishop DJ; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-018-0936-y). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-018-0936-y`. PMID: [29934848](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29934848/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Human training studies.
- **Verified extraction:** Volume may be critical for mitochondrial content; relative intensity for respiratory function. Content and function can dissociate. Acute signalling does not guarantee training adaptation.
- **Limitations:** Review preceding the 2025 meta-regression. Some claims are interpretive.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy25"></a>
<a id="int32"></a>
### PHY25 — Physiological adaptations to interval training and the role of exercise intensity

**MacInnis MJ, Gibala MJ; 2017.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1113/JP273196). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1113/JP273196`. PMID: [27748956](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27748956/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Human interval and continuous training studies.
- **Verified extraction:** Work-matched comparisons suggest greater mitochondrial content after HIIT than MICT. SIT can match MICT mitochondrial gains at lower volume. HIIT generally increases VO2max more than MICT for a given volume. Intensity-duration-frequency interactions not thoroughly explored.
- **Limitations:** Review. Not cyclist-only. Does not establish a Zone 2 pathway.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27748956/)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY25`, `INT32`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy26"></a>
### PHY26 — Superior mitochondrial adaptations in human skeletal muscle after interval compared to continuous single-leg cycling matched for total work

**MacInnis MJ, Zacharewicz E, Martin BJ, et al.; 2017.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1113/jp272570). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1113/jp272570`. PMID: [27396440](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27396440/).
- **Design / duration:** Within-subject training; 6 sessions over 2 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 10 young active men, single-leg cycling.
- **Verified extraction:** HIIT (4x5 min at 65% single-leg Wpeak) increased citrate synthase and mitochondrial respiration more than work- and duration-matched continuous cycling at 50% Wpeak.
- **Limitations:** Active men, not trained cyclists. Single-leg model. Short. Abstract truncated before exact percent changes.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy27"></a>
### PHY27 — Human skeletal muscle mitochondrial responses to single-leg intermittent or continuous cycle exercise training matched for absolute intensity and total work

**Skelly LE, MacInnis MJ, Bostad W, et al.; 2023.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.14332). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/sms.14332`. PMID: [36779702](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36779702/).
- **Design / duration:** Within-subject training; 12 sessions over 4 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 10 young healthy adults, 5 women.
- **Verified extraction:** When absolute intensity, time and volume were matched, interval pattern did not produce greater mitochondrial-content biomarkers than continuous cycling. Abstract truncated before exact null statistics.
- **Limitations:** Not trained cyclists. Intensity was moderate (~50% single-leg Wpeak), so this does not refute higher-intensity advantages.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy28"></a>
### PHY28 — The additional effect of training above the maximal metabolic steady state on VO2peak, Wpeak and time-trial performance in endurance-trained athletes

**Rosenblat MA, Arnold J, Nelson H, Watt J, Seiler S; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-023-01924-y). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-023-01924-y`. PMID: [37737543](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37737543/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; 2-12 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 14 studies, 171 recreational and 128 competitive endurance athletes, VO2peak 38-68.
- **Verified extraction:** Training above MMSS added 2.5 mL/kg/min (95% CI 1.4-3.6) to VO2peak versus training only below MMSS. No intensity-specific effect concluded for Wpeak or time trial, partly from smaller samples.
- **Limitations:** Mixed sports. Not a volume-dose trial. TT evidence underpowered by the authors' own statement.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy29"></a>
<a id="tid08"></a>
<a id="dur20"></a>
### PHY29 — The effect of training distribution, duration, and volume on VO2max and performance in trained cyclists: a systematic review, multilevel meta-analysis, and multivariate meta-regression

**Cove B, Chalmers S, Nelson MJ, Anderson M, Bennett H; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jsams.2024.12.005). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1016/j.jsams.2024.12.005`. PMID: [39788807](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39788807/).
- **Design / duration:** Multilevel meta-analysis and meta-regression; interventions >4 weeks; database search closed 23 October 2023; 2025 is the journal issue year.
- **Population / baseline:** Trained cyclists. Abstract: 41 studies, 81 groups, 797 participants. Inclusion was sex-specific Tier 3 study-mean VO2max (men >=59, women >=54.4 mL/kg/min), not every participant >=59..
- **Verified extraction:** Abstract point estimates only: training improved VO2max (g=0.42) and time-trial performance (g=0.39); no significant between-model difference. Confidence intervals are not copied because abstract and body intervals disagree. Longer duration was positively associated with both outcomes. No detected association of weekly or total volume with either outcome in the subsets that reported volume.
- **Limitations:** POL meant LIT or habitual training plus intervals shorter than 5 min above MLSS or at the highest sustainable intensity, not a measured 80/20 or below-LT1 distribution. Intervals longer than 5 min were non-polarized. Counts are internally inconsistent (abstract 41 studies; discussion 44; 23 randomized plus 21 non-randomized). Do not use body confidence intervals. Do not copy the reported weekly volume 4.9 plus or minus 7.3 h (range 2.6-14.3): that SD is implausible for the range. Certainty low. Null volume regression is not a causal optimum or proof that extra hours cannot help.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39788807/), [source 2](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jsams.2024.12.005), [source 3](https://cdck-file-uploads-global.s3.dualstack.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/trainerroad/original/3X/4/c/4c4615506951980ed84df665545b0fc04dd83829.pdf), [source 4](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39788807)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY29`, `TID08`, `DUR20`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy30"></a>
<a id="tid35"></a>
### PHY30 — Best-practice training characteristics within Olympic endurance sports as described by Norwegian world-class coaches

**Sandbakk O, Tonnessen E, Sandbakk SB, Losnegard T, Seiler S, Haugen T; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1186/s40798-025-00848-3). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1186/s40798-025-00848-3`. PMID: [40278987](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40278987/).
- **Design / duration:** Qualitative multiple-case study; coach-described annual cycle; data collection 2022; published 2025-04-25.
- **Population / baseline:** 12 male Norwegian world-class coaches; one road-cycling coach. Medal record >380 international medals across sports.
- **Verified extraction:** Road cycling high-load preparation weeks described as 7-10 sessions, 20-24 h total, 19-23 h endurance, 2-3 intensive days, 90-180 min intensive. Competition weeks 7-9 sessions, 18-22 h. One daily ride of 3-6 h. One-versus-two sessions per day untested. Common emphasis on high LIT volume and 2-3 key days.
- **Limitations:** One cycling informant. Coach report, not a trial. Zone scale is not Coggan or the three-zone research model. 30 h quote in the paper is not the road-cycling row.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)

<a id="dur24"></a>
### PHY31 — What is best practice for training intensity and duration distribution in endurance athletes?

**Seiler S; 2010.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.5.3.276). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.5.3.276`. PMID: [20861519](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20861519/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative synthesis of descriptive and intensification studies; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** National/international endurance athletes, typically 10-13 sessions/week.
- **Verified extraction:** Successful athletes converge near 80% low-intensity sessions and ~20% sessions dominated by high-intensity work. Intensification studies in the already well trained do not convincingly show long-term gains from more HIT emphasis.
- **Limitations:** Review/synthesis. Not an interval-format trial. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)

<a id="tid01"></a>
### PHY32 — Quantifying training intensity distribution in elite endurance athletes: is there evidence for an optimal distribution?

**Seiler KS, Kjerland GO; 2006.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1600-0838.2004.00418.x). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/j.1600-0838.2004.00418.x`. PMID: [16430681](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16430681/).
- **Design / duration:** Observational training quantification; 32 days, 384 sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 11 male nationally competitive junior cross-country skiers.
- **Verified extraction:** Heart-rate distribution about 75% zone 1, 8% zone 2, 17% zone 3, using VT1 and VT2. Session RPE similar. Athletes performed little training at lactate-threshold intensity.
- **Limitations:** Skiers, not cyclists. Descriptive, not an optimum trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)

<a id="phy33"></a>
<a id="tid25"></a>
### PHY33 — Autonomic recovery after exercise in trained athletes: intensity and duration effects

**Seiler S, Haugen O, Kuffel E; 2007.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0b013e318060f17d). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/mss.0b013e318060f17d`. PMID: [17762370](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17762370/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute crossover; single sessions; HRV followed 4 h.
- **Population / baseline:** 9 highly trained male runners (VO2max 72±5, 14±3 h/week) and 8 trained runners (VO2max 60±5, 7±1 h/week).
- **Verified extraction:** In highly trained runners, HRV recovered in 5-10 min after 60 or 120 min below VT1. Threshold and above-VT2 sessions delayed recovery to about 30 min, similarly. Less-trained runners were slower after the same intervals (>=90 min).
- **Limitations:** Runners, small n, acute autonomic marker, no 4 h arm, not an adaptation trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:17762370&resultType=core&format=json)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY33`, `TID25`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy34"></a>
### PHY34 — The road to gold: training and peaking characteristics in the year prior to a gold medal endurance performance

**Tonnessen E, Sylta O, Haugen TA, Hem E, Svendsen IS, Seiler S; 2014.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0101796). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1371/journal.pone.0101796`. PMID: [25019608](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25019608/).
- **Design / duration:** Retrospective training logs; 1 year before career-best competition.
- **Population / baseline:** 11 elite XC skiers and biathletes, 4 men VO2max 85±5, 7 women 73±3.
- **Verified extraction:** About 800 h and 500 sessions per year; ~500 h sport-specific. About 90% of aerobic time below the first lactate threshold. About 23% of sessions categorized as HIT. HIT volume stayed fairly stable; total volume followed traditional periodization.
- **Limitations:** Not cyclists. Self-report. 800 h/year is not a weekly peak.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy35"></a>
<a id="tid16"></a>
<a id="mon21"></a>
### PHY35 — Training characteristics of male and female professional road cyclists: a 4-year retrospective analysis

**van Erp T, Sanders D, de Koning JJ; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2019-0320). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2019-0320`. PMID: [31722298](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31722298/).
- **Design / duration:** Retrospective observational; multi-year team monitoring.
- **Population / baseline:** 20 male and 10 female professional cyclists, 4 years.
- **Verified extraction:** Men had higher distance, duration, kilojoules and mean power (d=0.44-1.98) and more time in low-intensity zones. Session TSS/TRIMP differences were trivial (d=0.07-0.12). Load per kilometre was moderately higher in women (d=0.67-0.76). Women appeared to compensate lower volume with higher intensity.
- **Limitations:** Abstract does not report weekly hours. One professional programme. Not an experiment.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)

- **Citation aliases:** `PHY35`, `TID16`, `MON21`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy36"></a>
### PHY36 — Differences in training characteristics between junior, under-23 and professional cyclists

**Gallo G, Leo P, March MM, et al.; 2022.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1055/a-1847-5414). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1055/a-1847-5414`. PMID: [35533684](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35533684/).
- **Design / duration:** Retrospective observational; one competitive season.
- **Population / baseline:** 30 male cyclists, junior, U23 and professional, 2019 season.
- **Verified extraction:** Retrieved abstract: professional session duration 2.6±0.3 h versus U23 2.2±0.3 h. Juniors spent more time in medium and high heart-rate zones than U23 and professionals. Other load figures were truncated and are not used.
- **Limitations:** Abstract incomplete. Not weekly hours.

<a id="phy37"></a>
### PHY37 — Training, performance, and physiological predictors of a successful elite senior career in junior competitive road cyclists

**Svendsen IS, Tonnessen E, Tjelta LI, Orn S; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2017-0824). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2017-0824`. PMID: [29745739](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29745739/).
- **Design / duration:** Retrospective cohort; junior competition exposure.
- **Population / baseline:** 80 male cyclists at age 18, later classified at age 23: 9 WorldTour, 24 continental, 26 club, 21 retired.
- **Verified extraction:** Future WorldTour riders competed more at age 18 (91.5±19.1 h) than club (62.8±21.8 h) or retired riders (61.8±23.4 h). Abstract conclusion mentions high training and competition volume; the verified numeric result is competition hours.
- **Limitations:** Competition hours, not a controlled training-volume effect. Small WorldTour n.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy38"></a>
### PHY38 — A critical evaluation of current methods for exercise prescription in women and men

**Iannetta D, Inglis EC, Mattu AT, et al.; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0000000000002147). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/mss.0000000000002147`. PMID: [31479001](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31479001/).
- **Design / duration:** Cross-sectional laboratory comparison; ramp plus 30-min constant-load trials.
- **Population / baseline:** 100 adults, 46 women, 54 men; VO2max 3.06±0.41 and 4.10±0.56 L/min.
- **Verified extraction:** LT at 45-74% VO2max and MLSS at 69-96% VO2max. Fixed %VO2max, %WRpeak and %HRmax scattered individuals across domains.
- **Limitations:** Not specifically trained cyclists. Shows prescription error, not a training outcome.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy39"></a>
### PHY39 — Variability in exercise tolerance and physiological responses to exercise prescribed relative to physiological thresholds and to maximum oxygen uptake

**Meyler S, Bottoms L, Wellsted D, Muniz-Pumares D; 2023.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1113/ep090878). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1113/ep090878`. PMID: [36710454](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36710454/).
- **Design / duration:** Repeated-measures laboratory; acute bouts.
- **Population / baseline:** 10 individuals.
- **Verified extraction:** Threshold-based prescription, especially critical power, reduced variability versus fixed %VO2max. All completed heavy-threshold bouts; only 30% completed heavy bouts prescribed by %VO2max, and 70% of those work rates exceeded CP.
- **Limitations:** Small n. Acute tolerance, not longitudinal adaptation. Population training status not fully specified in the retrieved abstract.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC10103872)

### PHY40 — Functional threshold power in cyclists: validity of the concept and physiological responses

**Borszcz FK, Tramontin AF, Bossi AH, Carminatti LJ, Costa VP; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1055/s-0044-101546). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1055/s-0044-101546`. PMID: [29801189](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29801189/).
- **Design / duration:** Validity study; incremental, 20- and 60-min trials, time to exhaustion.
- **Population / baseline:** 23 trained male cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** FTP20, FTP60 and individual anaerobic threshold not different on average, large correlations, but limits of agreement from about -40 to +32 W up to -62 to +60 W. Time to exhaustion at FTP20 50.9±15.7 min.
- **Limitations:** Men only. IAT is not MLSS or CP.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md), [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)

<a id="mon26"></a>
### PHY41 — Is the functional threshold power interchangeable with the maximal lactate steady state in trained cyclists?

**Borszcz FK, Tramontin AF, Costa VP; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2018-0572). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2018-0572`. PMID: [30676826](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30676826/).
- **Design / duration:** Validity study; incremental, FTP, constant-load MLSS series.
- **Population / baseline:** 15 cyclists, 7 trained and 8 well trained, VO2max 62.3±6.4, MAP 329±30 W.
- **Verified extraction:** FTP vs MLSS bias 1.4%±9.2% limits of agreement, TEE 4.7%, r=0.91. Agreement tighter in well-trained than trained riders.
- **Limitations:** Small n. Group validity is not individual interchangeability.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md), [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)

### PHY42 — Is the functional threshold power a valid surrogate of the lactate threshold?

**Valenzuela PL, Morales JS, Foster C, Lucia A, de la Villa P; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2018-0008). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2018-0008`. PMID: [29745765](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29745765/).
- **Design / duration:** Validity study; incremental plus 20-min trial.
- **Population / baseline:** 20 male cyclists split by fitness; recreational vs higher peak power.
- **Verified extraction:** Overall FTP 240±35 W vs LT 246±24 W, r=0.95, limits of agreement -2.4%±11.5%. Authors: better estimate in trained than recreational cyclists, where FTP may underestimate LT.

<a id="phy43"></a>
### PHY43 — Assessment of metabolic flexibility by means of measuring blood lactate, fat, and carbohydrate oxidation responses to exercise in professional endurance athletes and less-fit individuals

**San-Millan I, Brooks GA; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-017-0751-x). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-017-0751-x`. PMID: [28623613](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28623613/).
- **Design / duration:** Cross-sectional comparison; acute exercise tests.
- **Population / baseline:** Professional endurance athletes, moderately active individuals, and metabolic-syndrome patients.
- **Verified extraction:** Fat oxidation higher and blood lactate lower in professionals than in moderately active and metabolic-syndrome groups. Fat oxidation and lactate inversely correlated. Not a Zone 2 training trial.
- **Limitations:** Cross-sectional. Abstract truncated before all statistics. Does not isolate a training wattage.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy44"></a>
### PHY44 — Lactate threshold concepts: how valid are they?

**Faude O, Kindermann W, Meyer T; 2009.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.2165/00007256-200939060-00003). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.2165/00007256-200939060-00003`. PMID: [19453206](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19453206/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Endurance performance diagnosis.
- **Verified extraction:** Lactate thresholds can index endurance capacity but methods differ, and a fixed lactate concentration is not an individual threshold. Two-threshold aerobic-anaerobic transition framework is useful if the method is stated.
- **Limitations:** Review. Abstract truncated before a single recommended protocol.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy45"></a>
### PHY45 — Influence of exercise intensity and duration on biochemical adaptations in skeletal muscle

**Dudley GA, Abraham WM, Terjung RL; 1982.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1152/jappl.1982.53.4.844). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1152/jappl.1982.53.4.844`. PMID: [6295989](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/6295989/).
- **Design / duration:** Animal dose-response; 8 weeks, 5 days/week.
- **Population / baseline:** Rats, 19 treadmill protocols.
- **Verified extraction:** Cytochrome c rose with daily duration toward an intensity-dependent asymptote. Higher intensity reached the plateau in less daily time. Fast-twitch white fibres needed higher intensity.
- **Limitations:** Rats. Not transferable as a human FTP percentage.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy46"></a>
### PHY46 — No effect of a graded training program on the number of running-related injuries in novice runners

**Buist I, Bredeweg SW, van Mechelen W, et al.; 2008.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1177/0363546507307505). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1177/0363546507307505`. PMID: [17940147](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17940147/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomized controlled trial; 13-week graded programme vs 8-week standard.
- **Population / baseline:** 532 novice runners preparing for a 4-mile event.
- **Verified extraction:** 10% rule programme did not reduce injuries: 20.8% vs 20.3%, P=0.90.
- **Limitations:** Novice runners, not cyclists. Does not prove all progression rates are equal.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy47"></a>
### PHY47 — Excessive progression in weekly running distance and risk of running-related injuries

**Nielsen RO, Parner ET, Nohr EA, Sorensen H, Lind M, Rasmussen S; 2014.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.2519/jospt.2014.5164). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.2519/jospt.2014.5164`. PMID: [25155475](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25155475/).
- **Design / duration:** Prospective cohort; 1 year.
- **Population / baseline:** 874 healthy novice runners.
- **Verified extraction:** No statistically significant overall injury-rate difference across <10%, 10-30% and >30% weekly distance progression. Some distance-related injuries higher above 30%. Abstract truncated before all injury-specific estimates.
- **Limitations:** Novice runners. Exploratory. Not a cycling rule.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

### PHY48 — Is there evidence for an association between changes in training load and running-related injuries? A systematic review

**Damsted C, Glad S, Nielsen RO, Sorensen H, Malisoux L; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.26603/ijspt20180931). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.26603/ijspt20180931`. PMID: [30534459](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30534459/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Runners 18-65; 4 eligible articles.
- **Verified extraction:** Three of four articles found some association between load increases and running injury. Evidence base is small.
- **Limitations:** Running only. Does not validate a percentage rule.

<a id="mon13"></a>

**Impellizzeri FM, Tenan MS, Kempton T, Novak A, Coutts AJ; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2019-0864). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2019-0864`. PMID: [32502973](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32502973/).
- **Design / duration:** Methodological critique; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Training-load injury literature.

### PHY52 — Prevention, diagnosis, and treatment of the overtraining syndrome: joint consensus statement

**Meeusen R, Duclos M, Foster C, et al.; 2013.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0b013e318279a10a). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/MSS.0b013e318279a10a`. PMID: [23247672](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23247672/).
- **Design / duration:** Consensus statement; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Athletes.
- **Verified extraction:** Functional overreaching can precede supercompensation; non-functional overreaching and overtraining syndrome reflect inadequate recovery. No single diagnostic marker. Exclude illness and low energy availability.
- **Limitations:** Consensus. Does not define a cycling volume ceiling.

<a id="phy53"></a>
### PHY53 — Blood volume: its adaptation to endurance training

**Convertino VA; 1991.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/00005768-199112000-00004). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/00005768-199112000-00004`. PMID: [1798375](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1798375/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Endurance-training literature of the time.
- **Verified extraction:** Plasma volume accounts for nearly all exercise-induced hypervolemia for about 2-4 weeks; thereafter expansion may be shared equally with red-cell volume.
- **Limitations:** 1991 review. Not a modern CO-rebreathing cyclist cohort.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy54"></a>
### PHY54 — The influence of exercise volume and posture on exercise-induced plasma volume expansion

**Nelson WB, Walker JM, Hansen C, et al.; 2023.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.14814/phy2.15601). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.14814/phy2.15601`. PMID: [36802178](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36802178/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute crossover; 24 h after interval sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 10 subjects per comparison.
- **Verified extraction:** Plasma volume +7.3±4.4% after treadmill and +6.3±3.5% after cycle intervals. Four, six and eight intervals produced similar expansion (6.6±4.0%, 4.7±2.6%, 4.2±5.6%).
- **Limitations:** Acute, not training adaptation. Not trained cyclists specified in the retrieved abstract.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy55"></a>
<a id="int44"></a>
### PHY55 — Heavy-, severe-, and extreme-, but not moderate-intensity exercise increase VO2max and thresholds after 6 wk of training

**Inglis EC, Iannetta D, Rasica L, et al.; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/MSS.0000000000003406). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/MSS.0000000000003406`. PMID: [38376995](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38376995/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomized six-group training trial; 6 weeks, 3 sessions/week.
- **Population / baseline:** 84 young healthy adults, 42 women and 42 men, 14 per group. Not described as trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Versus control VO2max change 0.1±1.2 mL/kg/min, moderate-domain change 1.8±2.7 was not statistically different from control, nor was the estimated lactate-threshold change. Lower heavy 3.3±2.4, upper heavy 5.4±2.3, severe HIIT 6.2±2.8 and sprint 4.7±2.3 showed a between-group increase versus control. Work-matched except sprints. No clear between-group benefit is not proof of ineffectiveness.
- **Limitations:** Untrained/recreational young adults. Not 55 vs 75% FTP. Not high-volume LIT over months. Does not prove moderate riding never adapts other qualities.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38376995/)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY55`, `INT44`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy56"></a>
<a id="dur23"></a>
### PHY56 — What is zone 2 training? Experts' viewpoint on definition, training methods, and expected adaptations

**Sitko S, Artetxe X, Bonnevie-Svendsen M, et al.; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2024-0303). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2024-0303`. PMID: [40010355](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40010355/).
- **Design / duration:** Expert commentary; n/a; online 2025-02-26.
- **Population / baseline:** 14 applied sport scientists and professional coaches.
- **Verified extraction:** Consensus that zone 2 should preferably be immediately below LT1 or VT1, via continuous, variable or interval sessions. Expected central and peripheral adaptations might also occur slightly above or below that intensity.
- **Limitations:** Opinion, not a trial. Abstract does not state a minimum session duration or an FTP percentage. Do not treat panel preference as experimental proof.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)

<a id="phy57"></a>
<a id="dur57"></a>
### PHY57 — Comparison of acute physiological responses between one long and two short sessions of moderate-intensity training in endurance athletes

**Kjosen Talsnes R, Torvik PO, Skovereng K, Sandbakk O; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.3389/fphys.2024.1428536). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.3389/fphys.2024.1428536`. PMID: [39139482](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39139482/).
- **Design / duration:** Counterbalanced crossover, acute; one day of 6x10 min vs two bouts of 3x10 min separated by 6.5 h; running.
- **Population / baseline:** 14 male endurance athletes, VO2max 69.2±4.2.
- **Verified extraction:** Single session produced second-half drift in HR, lactate and RPE, higher supine HR in the next hour, and higher session RPE. Second short session had lower HR and lactate than the first. Authors state training-intervention studies are still required.
- **Limitations:** Running, not cycling. Moderate intervals, not 4 h easy riding. Acute only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC11319183), [source 2](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39139482)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY57`, `DUR57`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy58"></a>
### PHY58 — Influence of endurance training during childhood on total hemoglobin mass

**Prommer N, Wachsmuth N, Thieme I, et al.; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.3389/fphys.2018.00251). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.3389/fphys.2018.00251`. PMID: [29618981](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29618981/).
- **Design / duration:** Longitudinal plus cross-sectional; up to 3.5 years.
- **Population / baseline:** 17 endurance-trained children aged 9.7±1.3 followed up to 3.5 years; 18 untrained controls.
- **Verified extraction:** Hb mass rose ~50% and tracked lean mass (r=0.959). Training >4 h/week associated with +25.4 g versus low-volume peers, overlapping age effects. About 3.6 mL/min VO2max per gram Hb (r=0.939).
- **Limitations:** Children. Growth confounded. Not adult cyclists.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

<a id="phy59"></a>
### PHY59 — The Tour de France, also possible for mortals? A comparison of a recreational and a World Tour cyclist

**Barranco-Gil D, Muriel X, Lucia A, Joyner MJ, DeSouza CA, Valenzuela PL; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1152/japplphysiol.00798.2023). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1152/japplphysiol.00798.2023`. PMID: [38174376](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38174376/).
- **Design / duration:** Case comparison; 2023 Tour de France, 21 stages.
- **Population / baseline:** One 58-year-old recreational man, estimated VO2max 45.4, and one WorldTour man, 80.5 mL/kg/min.
- **Verified extraction:** Recreational rider finished in 191 h at 1.50 W/kg; professional in 87 h at 3.45 W/kg. Estimated daily energy expenditure was high in both. Not a training-volume prescription.
- **Limitations:** n=2. Illustrates demand difference, not how to train.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md)

### PHY60 — Training characteristics of male and female WorldTour professional road cyclists before the competitive phase

**Mateo-March M, et al.; 2026.** [Original source](https://europepmc.org/article/PMC/PMC13217385). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.5114/biolsport.2026.156234`. PMID: [42220588](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42220588/).
- **Design / duration:** Observational training-log comparison; 10 weeks before the first competition of the season; published 23 February 2026.
- **Population / baseline:** 16 female (age 26±5) and 16 male (age 29±6) WorldTour road cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Sessions 5.9±0.9 vs 6.0±0.9 per week. Hours 16.7±2.6 vs 19.1±2.7 (p=0.016). Week-9 peak 20.1±3.2 h and 22.5±2.8 h; week-10 taper 11.2±4.6 h and 17.7±4.9 h. More than 90% pyramidal. LIT defined as below an assumed 70% of estimated CP, not measured LT1. By power, LIT was 59.7±10.4% of time in women and 70.4±6.2% in men.
- **Limitations:** One pre-competitive block, not a full season. LIT/MIT boundary assumed from a meta-analysis (70% CP), not individual LT1. Not a training intervention.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC13217385/fullTextXML), [source 2](https://europepmc.org/article/PMC/PMC13217385)
- **Citation aliases:** `PHY60`, `TID15`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="phy61"></a>
<a id="tid09"></a>
### PHY61 — The effectiveness of polarized versus high-intensity training in moderately trained cyclists: a randomized parallel-design study

**Moran-MacDonald A, Wilson S, Wu Z, Haines G, Beaupre L, Aube J, Andrews C, Jonsson N, Gurd BJ; 2026.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-026-06380-w). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-026-06380-w`. PMID: [42560532](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42560532/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomized parallel added-volume trial; 6 weeks; first published 2026-08-06.
- **Population / baseline:** 19 moderately trained cyclists/triathletes, 17 men; baseline VO2max not reported in the retrieved abstract.
- **Verified extraction:** Three weekly HIIT days with versus without 240 min/week of moderate-domain cycling. Both groups reduced TT time by an average 8.5±1.2%. No time×group interaction for TT, peak work rate, LT1 or LT2 despite about 23 extra hours. No VO2max change reported in the retrieved abstract.
- **Limitations:** Small, short, mostly male and moderately trained; total load not matched. Nonsignificance is not equivalence or a long-term dose optimum. Does not test a 6/8/10/12 h dose curve. The abstract's internally questionable exploratory eta-squared/power statement is not interpreted.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Aerobic fitness, volume and easy intensity](../topics/physiology-volume-endurance.md), [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-026-06380-w), [source 2](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID%3A42560532%20AND%20SRC%3AMED&format=json&resultType=core&pageSize=1), [source 3](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:42560532&resultType=core&format=json), [source 4](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42560532/)

### TID03 — From heart-rate data to training quantification: a comparison of 3 methods of training-intensity analysis

**Sylta Ø, Tønnessen E, Seiler S; 2014.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2013-0298). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2013-0298`. PMID: [24408353](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24408353/).
- **Design / duration:** Method comparison; ~14-day camp, 570 sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 29 elite XC skiers, 16 male 13 female, VO2max 76±7.
- **Verified extraction:** Time-in-zone, session-goal, and hybrid methods are not interchangeable; conversion factors are provided.

<a id="dur18"></a>
### TID04 — Six weeks of a polarized training-intensity distribution leads to greater physiological and performance adaptations than a threshold model in trained cyclists

**Neal CM et al.; 2013.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1152/japplphysiol.00652.2012). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1152/japplphysiol.00652.2012`. PMID: [23264537](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23264537/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomized crossover; 2 x 6 weeks, 4 weeks detraining between.
- **Population / baseline:** 12 male cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** POL 6.4±1.4 h/wk 80/0/20 vs THR 7.5±2.0 h/wk 57/43/0. Greater POL gains in PPO 8±2% vs 3±1%, LT 9±3% vs 2±4%, high-intensity capacity 85±14% vs 37±14%. Both improved 40 km TT; superior 40 km effect not stated. No mitochondrial enzyme change.
- **Limitations:** Small male sample; volumes not perfectly matched; abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:23264537&resultType=core&format=json), [source 2](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/23264537)
