<a id="dur19"></a>
### TID05 — Polarized vs. threshold training intensity distribution on endurance sport performance: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials

**Rosenblat MA, Perrotta AS, Vicenzino B; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/jsc.0000000000002618). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/jsc.0000000000002618`. PMID: [29863593](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29863593/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; search 6 Nov 2016; interventions in included trials.
- **Population / baseline:** Endurance-trained athletes, VO2max/peak >50, >2 years training.
- **Verified extraction:** 4 studies qualitative, 3 in TT meta-analysis; PEDro 4-5/10. TT ES -0.66 (95% CI -1.17 to -0.15) favoring POL over THR.
- **Limitations:** Few studies, low quality scores, not pyramidal comparison, not cyclist-only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:29863593&resultType=core&format=json), [source 2](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/29863593)
- **Citation aliases:** `TID05`, `DUR19`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="tid06"></a>
### TID06 — Comparison of polarized versus other types of endurance training intensity distribution on athletes' endurance performance: a systematic review with meta-analysis

**Silva Oliveira P, Boppre G, Fonseca H; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-024-02034-z). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-024-02034-z`. PMID: [38717713](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38717713/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review with meta-analysis; search to 20 Oct 2022; interventions ≥4 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** Adults/young adults in endurance TID trials, n=437 across 17 studies.
- **Verified extraction:** VO2peak SMD 0.24 (0.01 to 0.48); TT SMD -0.01 (-0.28 to 0.25); TTE and VT2/LT2 speed/power not different. VO2peak edge in <12 weeks (SMD 0.40, n=163) and highly trained (SMD 0.46, n=125).
- **Limitations:** Other TIDs pooled; small VO2peak effect; RCT risk of bias some concerns.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC11329428/fullTextXML), [source 2](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-024-02034-z)

<a id="tid07"></a>
### TID07 — Which training intensity distribution intervention will produce the greatest improvements in maximal oxygen uptake and time-trial performance in endurance athletes? A systematic review and network meta-analysis of individual participant data

**Rosenblat MA et al.; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-024-02149-3). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-024-02149-3`. PMID: [39888556](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39888556/).
- **Design / duration:** IPD network meta-analysis; search to 11 Feb 2024; 13 studies.
- **Population / baseline:** 348 endurance athletes (296 male, 52 female); 150 recreational, 198 competitive.
- **Verified extraction:** Time-in-HR-zone: POL vs PYR VO2max SMD -0.06 p=0.68; TT SMD -0.05 p=0.34. No significant POL advantage vs other TIDs. Subgroup: competitive may gain more VO2max from POL and recreational from PYR (SMD -0.63, p<0.05).
- **Limitations:** Abstract only; not cyclist-only; nonsignificance is not equivalence.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:39888556&resultType=core&format=json), [source 2](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39888556/)

**Esteve-Lanao J, Foster C, Seiler S, Lucia A; 2007.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/r-19725.1). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/r-19725.1`. PMID: [17685689](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17685689/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomized training comparison; 5 months.
- **Population / baseline:** 12 sub-elite runners.
- **Verified extraction:** Zone 3 matched ~8%. More zone 1 (80.5/11.8/8.3) improved 10.4 km more than more zone 2 (66.8/24.7/8.5): -157±13 s vs -121.5±7.1 s, p=0.03.
- **Limitations:** Runners, n=12, not cyclists.

**Muñoz I, Seiler S, Bautista J, España J, Larumbe E, Esteve-Lanao J; 2014.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2012-0350). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Randomized 10-week training; 10 weeks.

- **Design / duration:** Controlled preparation comparison; 11 weeks.

DOI: `10.3389/fphys.2014.00033`. PMID: [24550842](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24550842/).

### TID17 — The effect of different high-intensity periodization models on endurance adaptations

**Sylta Ø et al.; 2016.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0000000000001007). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/mss.0000000000001007`. PMID: [27300278](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27300278/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomized 3-group, load-matched; 12 weeks, 24 interval sessions plus high LIT.
- **Population / baseline:** 63 cyclists, VO2peak 61.3±5.8.
- **Verified extraction:** Increasing was 4x16 then 4x8 then 4x4 (mean session lactate about 4.7, 9.2, and 12.7 mmol/L: longer and lower intensity first). Decreasing was the opposite (shorter, higher intensity first). Mixed alternated formats. All improved about 5-10% in 40-min power, peak power, and VO2peak. No 12-week group difference. Neither order isolates a pure threshold-then-VO2 or VO2-then-threshold sequence.
- **Limitations:** Candidate for the prompt's sequencing claim; transcript not supplied. Abstract only. Do not map increasing to VO2-first or decreasing to threshold-first.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)

**Solli GS, Odden I, Sælen V, Hansen J, Mølmen KS, Rønnestad BR; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1002/ejsc.12223). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

<a id="dur14"></a>
### TID23 — Interval training program optimization in highly trained endurance cyclists

**Laursen PB, Shing CM, Peake JM, Coombes JS, Jenkins DG; 2002.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1097/00005768-200211000-00017). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1097/00005768-200211000-00017`. PMID: [12439086](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12439086/).
- **Design / duration:** 4-week randomized HIT comparison plus control; 4 weeks, HIT twice weekly.
- **Population / baseline:** 38 cyclists and triathletes, VO2peak 64.5±5.2.
- **Verified extraction:** All HIT groups improved 40 km TT +4.4 to +5.8% and peak power +3.0 to +6.2% vs control. Groups using 8x60% Tmax at Pmax improved VO2peak more than control (+5.4% and +8.1% vs +1.0%). 12x30 s at 175% PPO also improved TT. Abstract duplicates a group label, so recovery-mode assignment is unclear.
- **Limitations:** Abstract labeling error. Short intervention. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:12439086&resultType=core&format=json), [source 2](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12439086/), [source 3](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/12439086)
- **Citation aliases:** `TID23`, `INT15`, `DUR14`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="tid24"></a>
### TID24 — Cardiac parasympathetic reactivation following exercise: implications for training prescription

**Stanley J, Peake JM, Buchheit M; 2013.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-013-0083-4). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-013-0083-4`. PMID: [23912805](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23912805/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; not a new trial.
- **Population / baseline:** Endurance training literature.
- **Verified extraction:** Autonomic recovery summarized as up to 24 h after low intensity, 24-48 h after threshold, at least 48 h after high intensity. Fitter athletes recover faster.
- **Limitations:** Marker review, not a performance dose-response.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:23912805&resultType=core&format=json)

<a id="tid26"></a>
### TID26 — Nonconsecutive- versus consecutive-day high-intensity interval training in cyclists

**Gross M, Swensen T, King D; 2007.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0b013e3180cac209). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/mss.0b013e3180cac209`. PMID: [17805101](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17805101/).
- **Design / duration:** Group comparison; 3 weeks, 3 HIT sessions/week.
- **Population / baseline:** 15 trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Consecutive (n=9) and nonconsecutive (n=6) schedules both improved VO2peak, peak aerobic power, and 5 km TT, with no between-group difference in the authors' conclusion.
- **Limitations:** Small, short; not equivalence. Unverified secondary percentages not used.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:17805101&resultType=core&format=json)

<a id="tid27"></a>
### TID27 — Effects of tapering on performance: a meta-analysis

**Bosquet L, Montpetit J, Arvisais D, Mujika I; 2007.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0b013e31806010e0). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/mss.0b013e31806010e0`. PMID: [17762369](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17762369/).
- **Design / duration:** Meta-analysis; pre-event tapers.
- **Population / baseline:** Competitive athletes, 27 datasets.
- **Verified extraction:** Larger effects with ~2 weeks and exponential volume cuts of about 41-60%, without cutting intensity or frequency.
- **Limitations:** Heterogeneous sports; subgroup 'optima' are not a factorial law; not a mid-build deload trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:17762369&resultType=core&format=json)

<a id="tid28"></a>
### TID28 — Functional overreaching: the key to peak performance during the taper?

**Aubry A et al.; 2014.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0000000000000301). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/mss.0000000000000301`. PMID: [25134000](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25134000/).
- **Design / duration:** Controlled overload and taper; 8 weeks including overload and 4-week taper.
- **Population / baseline:** 33 trained male triathletes.
- **Verified extraction:** Functionally overreached athletes supercompensated less than acutely fatigued athletes (~2.6%) and had more infections (70% vs 20%).
- **Limitations:** Male triathletes; qualitative statistics.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:25134000&resultType=core&format=json)

<a id="tid29"></a>
### TID29 — HIT maintains performance during the transition period and improves next season performance in well-trained cyclists

**Rønnestad BR, Askestad A, Hansen J; 2014.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-014-2919-5). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-014-2919-5`. PMID: [24878691](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24878691/).
- **Design / duration:** Group comparison; 8-week transition.
- **Population / baseline:** 13 well-trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** One HIT every 7-10 days plus LIT (n=7) vs LIT emphasis (n=6). Magnitude-based inference favored HIT for power at 4 mmol and 40-min performance.
- **Limitations:** Tiny sample; MBI; not a universal minimum.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:24878691&resultType=core&format=json)

### TID30 — Reduced training frequencies and maintenance of increased aerobic power

**Hickson RC, Rosenkoetter MA; 1981.** [Original source](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/7219129/). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Reduced-training experiment after a build; 15 weeks reduced frequency after 10 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** Young adults, recently trained, n=12.
- **Verified extraction:** VO2max maintained at 2 or 4 days/week if intensity and duration kept.
- **Limitations:** Not trained cyclists.

### TID31 — Reduced training intensities and loss of aerobic power, endurance, and cardiac growth

**Hickson RC et al.; 1985.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1152/jappl.1985.58.2.492). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1152/jappl.1985.58.2.492`. PMID: [3156841](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/3156841/).
- **Design / duration:** Reduced-intensity experiment; 15 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** Recently trained young adults.
- **Verified extraction:** Cutting intensity by one-third or two-thirds lost VO2max gains despite maintained frequency and duration.
- **Limitations:** Not athletes in season.

<a id="tid32"></a>
### TID32 — Time course of loss of adaptations after stopping prolonged intense endurance training

**Coyle EF et al.; 1984.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1152/jappl.1984.57.6.1857). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1152/jappl.1984.57.6.1857`. PMID: [6511559](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/6511559/).
- **Design / duration:** Detraining time course; 84 days of inactivity.
- **Population / baseline:** 7 endurance-trained subjects who stopped.
- **Verified extraction:** VO2max -7% by 21 days and -16% by 56 days. Enzyme half-time ~12 days. Capillarization did not decline in the observed window.
- **Limitations:** Complete cessation, not reduced training.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:6511559&resultType=core&format=json)

<a id="tid33"></a>
### TID33 — Maintaining physical performance: the minimal dose of exercise needed to preserve endurance and strength over time

**Spiering BA, Mujika I, Sharp MA, Foulis SA; 2021.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/jsc.0000000000003964). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/jsc.0000000000003964`. PMID: [33629972](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33629972/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; up to 15 weeks in cited maintenance studies.
- **Population / baseline:** General and military populations.
- **Verified extraction:** Endurance can be maintained with frequency as low as 2 sessions/week or volume cut 33-66% if intensity is maintained.
- **Limitations:** Not a trained-cyclist prescription.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:33629972&resultType=core&format=json)

### TID34 — Training prescription guided by heart-rate variability in cycling

**Javaloyes A; Sarabia JM; Lamberts RP; Moya-Ramon M; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2018-0122). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2018-0122`. PMID: [29809080](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29809080/).
- **Design / duration:** Controlled training comparison; 4 baseline + 8 training weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 17 well-trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** HRV-guided group improved PPO 5.1%±4.5%, W at VT2 13.9%±8.8%, 40-min TT 7.3%±4.5%. Traditional group did not improve significantly. Between-group differences not significant.
- **Limitations:** Abstract. Small. Magnitude-based inference used.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)

**Haugen T, Sandbakk Ø, Seiler S, Tønnessen E; 2022.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-022-01692-1). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Narrative integration of observational practice; training year.
- **Population / baseline:** World-class distance runners.

<a id="tid37"></a>
### TID37 — Influence of interval training frequency on time-trial performance in elite endurance athletes

**Tønnessen E, Hisdal J, Rønnestad BR; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.3390/ijerph17093190). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.3390/ijerph17093190`. PMID: [32375328](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32375328/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomized frequency comparison, volume matched; intervention period in-season preparation.
- **Population / baseline:** Elite XC skiers/biathletes; 4 short sessions n=6 vs 2 longer n=9.
- **Verified extraction:** Intervals ~85% HRmax. Two longer sessions improved economy and fractional utilization; four shorter did not show those changes. VO2max unchanged in both.
- **Limitations:** Tiny, mostly male, skiers; 85% HRmax is not necessarily severe-domain work.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:32375328&resultType=core&format=json)

**Billat VL et al.; 1999.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1097/00005768-199901000-00024). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1097/00005768-199901000-00024`. PMID: [9927024](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/9927024/).
- **Design / duration:** Sequential training phases; 4 weeks of 1 vVO2max session/week, then 4 weeks of 3/week.
- **Population / baseline:** 8 subjects.
- **Verified extraction:** One session/week improved velocity at VO2max. Three sessions/week did not further improve performance; end-test norepinephrine rose.

DOI: `10.14814/phy2.70573`. PMID: [40976973](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40976973/).
- **Design / duration:** Exploratory frequency comparison; 6 weeks of 4x4 once, twice, or thrice weekly.
- **Population / baseline:** 26 recreationally active adults, VO2max 51.3±7.1.

### TID47 — Aerobic high-intensity intervals improve VO2max more than moderate training

**Helgerud J, Høydal K, Wang E, et al.; 2007.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0b013e3180304570). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/mss.0b013e3180304570`. PMID: [17414804](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17414804/).
- **Design / duration:** 8-week randomized running comparison, work-matched; 8 weeks, 3 days/week.
- **Population / baseline:** 40 moderately trained men.
- **Verified extraction:** 4x4 at 90-95% HRmax raised VO2max 7.2% (55.5 to 60.4). 15/15 raised it 5.5% (60.5 to 64.4). Both beat lactate-threshold and 70% HRmax running. Stroke volume +~10% after interval training.
- **Limitations:** Running, not cycling. Moderately trained. 4x4 baseline lower than 15/15. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)

### TID48 — Adaptations to aerobic interval training: interactive effects of exercise intensity and total work duration

**Seiler S, Jøranson K, Olesen BV, Hetlelid KJ; 2013.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1600-0838.2011.01351.x). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/j.1600-0838.2011.01351.x`. PMID: [21812820](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21812820/).
- **Design / duration:** 7-week randomized comparison of exclusive interval durations; 7 weeks, 2 interval sessions/week.
- **Population / baseline:** 35 recreational cyclists, 29 men 6 women, VO2peak 52±6, ~6 h/week.
- **Verified extraction:** 4x8 min beat 4x4, 4x16, and low-only for VO2peak, power at VO2peak, and power at 4 mmol. 4x8 gain contrast 11.4% (8.0-14.9) vs 5.5% for 4x4 and 5.6% for 4x16. Performed at 90% HRpeak and 9.6 mmol/L vs 94%/13.2 for 4x4 and 88%/4.9 for 4x16.
- **Limitations:** Abstract only. Recreational, not well-trained. n=9 per interval arm.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md), [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)

<a id="tid49"></a>
### TID49 — A systematic review on the effectiveness of active recovery interventions on athletic performance

**Ortiz RO et al.; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/jsc.0000000000002589). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/jsc.0000000000002589`. PMID: [29742750](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29742750/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review; post-exercise active recovery.
- **Population / baseline:** competitive adult athletes.
- **Verified extraction:** Weak evidence that active recovery improves subsequent performance; psychological benefit more plausible.
- **Limitations:** Not a test of multi-hour easy rides.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)

<a id="tid50"></a>
### TID50 — Effects of tapering on performance in endurance athletes: a systematic review and meta-analysis

**Wang Z et al.; 2023.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0282838). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1371/journal.pone.0282838`. PMID: [37163550](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37163550/).
- **Design / duration:** Meta-analysis; pre-event tapers.
- **Population / baseline:** endurance athletes, 14 studies.
- **Verified extraction:** TT SMD -0.45. Volume cuts of 41-60% with intensity and frequency maintained, lasting up to 21 days, were associated with better TTs.
- **Limitations:** Mixes sports; pre-taper overload finding conflicts with Aubry on functional overreaching.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Distribution, periodization and recovery](../topics/distribution-periodization-recovery.md)

## VO₂-focused intervals

<a id="int01"></a>
### INT01 — The higher the fraction of maximal oxygen uptake is during interval training, the greater is the cycling performance gain

**Odden I, Nymoen L, Urianstad T, Kristoffersen M, Hammarström D, Hansen J, Mølmen KS, Rønnestad BR; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1002/ejsc.12202). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1002/ejsc.12202`. PMID: [39385317](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39385317/).
- **Design / duration:** 9-week training intervention; %VO2max measured every session; regression plus post-hoc median split, not randomized to high vs low fraction; 9 weeks, ~21 sessions of 5x8 min at PO40min, three work-rate patterns in randomized order.
- **Population / baseline:** 22 well-trained cyclists, 19 men 3 women, VO2max 67.1±6.4 mL/kg/min.
- **Verified extraction:** Mean interval %VO2max associated with gains in VO2max (R2adj 0.54, p=0.029; multivariate model including baseline, sex, mass change; slope 0.25 mL/kg/min per percentage point), Wmax (R2adj 0.44), power at 4 mmol (R2adj 0.25), and a performance index (R2adj 0.36). No association with 15-min power (p=0.215) or Hb mass (p=0.853). Post-hoc high vs low fraction: VO2max +5.23 vs +1.78 mL/kg/min. High-fraction half did more zone 1 (3:12 vs 1:48 h) and tended to train more total hours (9:43 vs 7:40). Time≥90% only tended to relate to VO2max change. RPE and lactate did not separate the halves.
- **Limitations:** Association not causation. Post-hoc split. Easy-volume imbalance. Three women. R2adj is not fraction-alone variance. Title overclaims.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC11534653/fullTextXML), [source 2](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39385317/), [source 3](https://doi.org/10.1002/ejsc.12202)

<a id="int02"></a>
### INT02 — The higher oxygen consumption during multiple short intervals is sex-independent and not influenced by skeletal muscle characteristics in well-trained cyclists

**Urianstad T, Hamarsland H, Odden I, Lorentzen HC, Hammarström D, Mølmen KS, Rønnestad BR; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1002/ejsc.12214). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1002/ejsc.12214`. PMID: [39435498](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39435498/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute randomized crossover, workload-matched; Single sessions: 6x8 min.
- **Population / baseline:** 11 well-trained women VO2max 62.5±6.4 and 8 men VO2max 81.0±5.2 mL/kg/min.
- **Verified extraction:** 30/15 at 118/60% PO40min produced higher mean %VO2max (86.7±10.1%) and longer time ≥90% (1123±787 s) than constant 100% (85.0±10.4%; 879±779 s) and 60/60 at 110/90% (85.6±10.0%; 917±745 s). No sex difference. Vastus lateralis fiber type, capillarization, and CS activity unrelated to %VO2max.
- **Limitations:** Acute only. Huge SD on time ≥90%. Mean %VO2max difference is 1-2 points.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC11534666/fullTextXML), [source 2](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39435498/)

<a id="int03"></a>
<a id="dur13"></a>
### INT03 — Short intervals induce superior training adaptations compared with long intervals in cyclists - an effort-matched approach

**Rønnestad BR, Hansen J, Vegge G, Tønnessen E, Slettaløkken G; 2015.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.12165). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/sms.12165`. PMID: [24382021](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24382021/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomized 10-week training comparison, effort-matched not power-matched; 10 weeks, 2 HIT sessions/week.
- **Population / baseline:** Trained-to-well-trained male competitive cyclists; 20 randomized, 9 short and 7 long completers.
- **Verified extraction:** SI: 3x13x30/15, 3 min between series. LI: 4x5 min, 2.5 min recovery. Recovery 50% of work power. Work power 363±32 vs 324±42 W. RPE and lactate similar. VO2max +8.7±5.0% vs +2.6±5.2% (p≤0.05). Wmax +8.5±5.2% vs +1.6±3.6%. Power at 4 mmol +12±9% vs +5±6% (between-group p=0.12). 40-min power +12±10% vs +4±4% (p=0.056). 5-min +8±7% vs +3±7% (ns between groups). 30-s Wingate +5±3% vs +1.4±3.7% (p=0.10). Authors infer time≥90% VO2max as cause but did not measure VO2 during training.
- **Limitations:** Male only. Small n. Dropouts. Effort matching confounds duration with power. Mechanism not measured in this trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.paulogentil.com/pdf/Short%20intervals%20induce%20superior%20training%20adaptations%20compared%20with%20long%20intervals%20in%20cyclists%20%E2%80%93%20An%20effort-matched%20approach.pdf), [source 2](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24382021/), [source 3](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/24382021)
- **Citation aliases:** `INT03`, `DUR13`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="int04"></a>
<a id="dur12"></a>
### INT04 — Superior performance improvements in elite cyclists following short-interval vs effort-matched long-interval training

**Rønnestad BR, Hansen J, Nygaard H, Lundby C; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.13627). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/sms.13627`. PMID: [31977120](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31977120/).
- **Design / duration:** 3-week randomized comparison, effort-matched by RPE; 3 weeks, 9 sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 18 elite cyclists, VO2max 73±4 mL/kg/min, n=9 per group.
- **Verified extraction:** 3x13x30/15 vs 4x5 min. Short intervals improved peak aerobic power (+3.7±4.3% vs -0.3±2.8%), fractional utilization and power at 4 mmol, and 20-min power (+4.7±4.4% vs -1.4±2.2%). No group difference in VO2max change.
- **Limitations:** Abstract only. Very short. Elite male-dominated sample. One lab.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31977120/), [source 2](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31977120)
- **Citation aliases:** `INT04`, `DUR12`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="int05"></a>
### INT05 — Systemic and muscular responses to effort-matched short intervals and long intervals in elite cyclists

**Almquist NW, Nygaard H, Vegge G, Hammarström D, Ellefsen S, Rønnestad BR; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.13672). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/sms.13672`. PMID: [32267032](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32267032/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute randomized crossover; Single sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 8 elite cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** 30 s vs 5 min, work:recovery 2:1. SI 14±3% higher mean power (421±27 vs 371±22 W) and longer time >90% VO2max and HRpeak, similar RPE, lactate, and EMG. Mostly similar mRNA; NHE1 decreased after SI.
- **Limitations:** Acute only. Large variance on time>90% (54±76% longer). Not an adaptation trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32267032/)

<a id="int06"></a>
### INT06 — Multiple short intervals induce longer time above 90% of maximal oxygen consumption than long intervals when matched by similar fixed intensity, but not during self-paced cycling ergometry

**Appelhans D, Rønnestad B, Skovereng K; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2024-0115). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2024-0115`. PMID: [40328438](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40328438/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute crossover, fixed %MAP and self-paced maximal; Single sessions: 3x13x30/15 vs 6x5 min.
- **Population / baseline:** 12 well-trained cyclists and triathletes, VO2max 68±6.3.
- **Verified extraction:** Time >90% VO2max greater for 30/15 than long intervals when fixed (p<0.01), not when self-paced (p=0.321). Self-paced raised time >90% from 420±259 to 1130±204 s (30/15) and 178±159 to 1016±312 s (long).
- **Limitations:** Abstract only. Acute. n=12.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40328438/)

<a id="int07"></a>
### INT07 — Optimizing interval training through power-output variation within the work intervals

**Bossi AH, Mesquida C, Passfield L, Rønnestad BR, Hopker JG; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2019-0260). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2019-0260`. PMID: [32244222](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32244222/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute crossover, matched duration and mean power; Two HIIT sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 14 cyclists, VO2max 69.2±6.6.
- **Verified extraction:** Varied-intensity work intervals: 410±207 s >90% VO2max vs 286±162 s constant (p=0.02). No difference in RPE, lactate, or perceptual/HR training load. Higher ventilation partly mediated.
- **Limitations:** Acute only. No adaptation follow-up.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)

<a id="int08"></a>
### INT08 — Similar time near VO2max regardless of work rate manipulation in cycling interval training

**Wommer D, Turnes T, Souza K, Guglielmo LGA; 2022.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1055/a-1550-9977). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1055/a-1550-9977`. PMID: [34261134](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34261134/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute crossover, work-matched 8x60 s; Single sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 10 healthy men, VO2max 3.82±0.52 L/min; not described as trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Constant, decreasing 110-90%, and increasing 90-110% of peak power: time ≥90% VO2max 274±132, 313±102, 310±113 s (p=0.37). Lactate and final RPE similar.
- **Limitations:** Acute. Small, not clearly trained. Different protocol from Bossi 2020.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)

<a id="int10"></a>
### INT10 — Effects of high-intensity training on physiological and hormonal adaptions in well-trained cyclists

**Sylta Ø, Tønnessen E, Sandbakk Ø, Hammarström D, Danielsen J, Skovereng K, Rønnestad BR, Seiler S; 2017.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/MSS.0000000000001214). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/MSS.0000000000001214`. PMID: [28121800](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28121800/).
- **Design / duration:** Time-course analysis of the Sylta 2016 intervention; 12 weeks, tests every 4 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** Same 63 well-trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** First 4 weeks of 4x16 (about 2-3 h/week interval work) improved power at 4 mmol and VO2peak more than starting with 4x4 (about 1 h/week). Groups converged by week 12.
- **Limitations:** Abstract only. Early difference confounded by accumulated work, not just bout length.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28121800/)

<a id="int11"></a>
### INT11 — How does interval-training prescription affect physiological and perceptual responses?

**Seiler S, Sylta Ø; 2017.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2016-0464). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2016-0464`. PMID: [28051345](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28051345/).
- **Design / duration:** Observational analysis of >1400 maximal-effort sessions inside a training study; 12 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 63 well-trained male cyclists, VO2peak 62±6.
- **Verified extraction:** 4x16, 4x8, 4x4 at maximal session effort were 95±5%, 106±5%, and 117±6% of 40-min TT power; peak HR 89, 91, 94% HRpeak; lactate 4.7, 9.2, 12.7 mmol/L. RPE 19-20 in 8% of 4x16 sessions vs 61% of 4x4.
- **Limitations:** Abstract only. Describes responses, not which format adapts more.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28051345/)

<a id="int14"></a>
<a id="dur15"></a>
### INT14 — Effects of different interval-training programs on cycling time-trial performance

**Stepto NK, Hawley JA, Dennis SC, Hopkins WG; 1999.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1097/00005768-199905000-00018). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1097/00005768-199905000-00018`. PMID: [10331896](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10331896/).
- **Design / duration:** 5-arm 3-week interval comparison plus usual base; 6 sessions in 3 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 20 male endurance cyclists, VO2peak 4.8±0.6 L/min, split across five protocols.
- **Verified extraction:** 8x4 min at 85% peak power (~2.8%) and 12x30 s at 175% peak power (~2.4%) improved 40 km TT. 12x60 s at 100% peak power and 4x8 min at 80% peak power did not significantly improve. Cells are small.
- **Limitations:** Abstract only. Fixed % peak power may under-dose longer bouts. Tiny cells.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md), [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10331896/), [source 2](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/10331896)
- **Citation aliases:** `INT14`, `DUR15`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

<a id="int16"></a>
### INT16 — The impact of rest duration on work intensity and RPE during interval training

**Seiler S, Hetlelid KJ; 2005.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/01.mss.0000177560.18014.d8). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/01.mss.0000177560.18014.d8`. PMID: [16177614](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16177614/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute crossover of rest duration; Single sessions, 6x4 min.
- **Population / baseline:** 9 well-trained male runners, VO2max 71±4.
- **Verified extraction:** Speed rose slightly from 1 to 2 min rest and no further at 4 min. Self-selected rest averaged 118±23 s.
- **Limitations:** Runners, not cyclists. Acute. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16177614/)

<a id="int17"></a>
### INT17 — Effect of work duration on physiological and RPE responses during self-paced interval training

**Seiler S, Sjursen JE; 2004.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1046/j.1600-0838.2003.00353.x). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1046/j.1600-0838.2003.00353.x`. PMID: [15387806](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15387806/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute Latin-square comparison of bout duration; Single sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 12 well-trained runners, VO2max 65±6.
- **Verified extraction:** 24x1, 12x2, 6x4, 4x6 min, 1:1 rest. Peak VO2 ~92% for 2-6 min vs 82±5% for 1 min. Peak RPE ~17 for all. Lactate similar.
- **Limitations:** Runners. Acute. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15387806/)

<a id="int18"></a>
### INT18 — Influence of recovery mode (passive vs. active) on time spent at maximal oxygen uptake during an intermittent session in young and endurance-trained athletes

**Thevenet D, Tardieu-Berger M, Berthoin S, Prioux J; 2007.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-006-0327-1). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-006-0327-1`. PMID: [17115178](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17115178/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute crossover; 30/30 runs to exhaustion.
- **Population / baseline:** 8 endurance-trained male adolescents, VO2max 57.4±6.1.
- **Verified extraction:** Passive recovery doubled time to exhaustion vs active at 50% MAV, with similar absolute time ≥90% and ≥95% VO2max.
- **Limitations:** Adolescents, running, acute. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17115178/)

<a id="int19"></a>
### INT19 — Effects of recovery mode (active vs. passive) on performance during a short high-intensity interval training program: a longitudinal study

**Ben Abderrahman A, Zouhal H, Chamari K, Thevenet D, et al.; 2013.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-012-2556-9). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-012-2556-9`. PMID: [23229881](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23229881/).
- **Design / duration:** 7-week running HIT, active vs passive recovery; 7 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 24 adults; training groups n=9 and n=9, control n=6.
- **Verified extraction:** VO2max increased in the active-recovery group, not the passive-recovery group, despite longer time to exhaustion with passive recovery.
- **Limitations:** Running, small, not cyclists. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23229881/)

<a id="int20"></a>
### INT20 — Effects of passive or active recovery regimes applied during long-term interval training on physical fitness

**Zouhal H, Abderrahman AB, Jayavel A, et al.; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1186/s40798-024-00673-0). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1186/s40798-024-00673-0`. PMID: [38443585](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38443585/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review, 24 studies, search to February 2023; Long-term interval programs.
- **Population / baseline:** Healthy trained and untrained, mixed sports.
- **Verified extraction:** Both active and passive recovery intervals improved fitness markers. Recovery type did not appear to determine outcomes.
- **Limitations:** Heterogeneous. Not cyclist-specific. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38443585/)

<a id="int21"></a>
### INT21 — Time spent at or near VO2max during high-intensity interval training: a systematic review and meta-analysis

**Schoenmakers P, Murray K, White J, Matta G, Bossi AH; 2026.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1186/s13102-026-01766-x). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1186/s13102-026-01766-x`. PMID: [42237396](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42237396/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis of acute tVO2max, search to September 2025; Acute sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 86 articles, 239 HIIT protocols, mixed.
- **Verified extraction:** Long work intervals (≥2 min) elicited more time near VO2max than short (≤30 s) or moderate bouts. Variable-intensity intervals beat even pacing (SMD 0.80). Active vs passive recovery: no effect. Authors state dose-response with long-term adaptation requires further investigation.
- **Limitations:** Acute synthesis. Short category is not specifically 30/15 series. Mixed populations. Opening full text plus abstract; subgroup tables not fully extracted.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC13480085/fullTextXML), [source 2](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42237396/)

### INT22 — Reliability of time spent near VO2max during 2 cycling interval training sessions: the effect of VO2max criteria

**Raimundo JAG, Tramontin AF, Martins EC, Borszcz FK, Turnes T, de Aguiar RA, Caputo F; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/JSC.0000000000005154). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/JSC.0000000000005154`. PMID: [40493484](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40493484/).
- **Design / duration:** Test-retest reliability; 5x2 min and 10x1 min sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 15 healthy men.
- **Verified extraction:** CV 19-35% for 5x2 min and 36-72% for 10x1 min. Same-day VO2max verification did not fix reliability.
- **Limitations:** Not trained cyclists. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40493484/)

<a id="int23"></a>
### INT23 — Time spent near VO2max during different cycling self-paced interval training protocols

**Dall'Agnol C, Turnes T, De Lucas RD; 2021.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2020-0314). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2020-0314`. PMID: [33771941](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33771941/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute randomized crossover; 4x4 and 8x? 8-min sessions with 4:1 or 2:1 work:recovery.
- **Population / baseline:** 11 cyclists, VO2max 55.7±7.4.
- **Verified extraction:** 4 min/2 min recovery and 8 min/4 min recovery prioritized time near VO2max. 4/2 mean power 275±23 W, higher than 4/1 (261±27 W).
- **Limitations:** Acute. Modestly trained. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)

<a id="int24"></a>
### INT24 — Interval training in the boundaries of severe domain: effects on aerobic parameters

**Turnes T, de Aguiar RA, Cruz RS, Caputo F; 2016.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-015-3263-0). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-015-3263-0`. PMID: [26373721](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26373721/).
- **Design / duration:** 4-week two-group training comparison; 4 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 21 recreational cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Upper severe-domain intervals had higher time at VO2max and larger VO2max gain (6.3±1.9% vs 3.3±1.8%) and lactate-threshold gain than 4x5 min at 105% critical power. Odden 2024 reports no individual correlation in this study; that null was not re-verified in Turnes full text.
- **Limitations:** Recreational. Domain confounded with time-at-VO2max. Abstract only for primary results.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)

**Rønnestad BR, Bjerkrheim KA, Hansen J, Mølmen KS; 2022.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.3389/fspor.2022.948127). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.3389/fspor.2022.948127`. PMID: [36439620](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36439620/).
- **Design / duration:** 6-day HIT block vs usual training; 6 days plus 5 days recovery.
- **Population / baseline:** 24 well-trained XC skiers, VO2max ~69.
- **Verified extraction:** Block improved maximal 1-min velocity and velocity at 4 mmol more than control. ICC of time ≥90% VO2max was 0.57.
- **Limitations:** Skiers, not cyclists. Abstract only. Correlation with adaptation not in the retrieved abstract.

<a id="int26"></a>
### INT26 — Effects of different protocols of high intensity interval training for VO2max improvements in adults: a meta-analysis

**Wen D, Utesch T, Wu J, et al.; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jsams.2019.01.013). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1016/j.jsams.2019.01.013`. PMID: [30733142](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30733142/).
- **Design / duration:** Meta-analysis of RCTs to April 2018; Varied.
- **Population / baseline:** 53 studies, healthy, overweight, and athletic adults; not trained-cyclist specific.
- **Verified extraction:** Long intervals ≥2 min, high volume ≥15 min, and ≥4-12 weeks had larger VO2max effects than short/low-volume HIIT when compared with control or MICT. Short intervals still beat control, especially in general populations.
- **Limitations:** Mixed populations. Abstract only. Not a refutation of effort-matched 30/15 in trained cyclists.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)

**Milanović Z, Sporiš G, Weston M; 2015.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-015-0365-0). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-015-0365-0`. PMID: [26243014](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26243014/).
- **Design / duration:** Meta-analysis; ≥2 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 28 studies, 723 adults, baseline VO2max 40.8±7.9.
- **Verified extraction:** HIT +5.5 mL/kg/min vs control; small additional benefit vs continuous training. Low baseline fitness. Not transferable as a magnitude to trained cyclists.
- **Limitations:** Untrained/recreational baseline. Abstract only.

**Rosenblat MA, Perrotta AS, Thomas SG; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-020-01264-1). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-020-01264-1`. PMID: [32034701](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32034701/).
- **Design / duration:** Meta-analysis, 6 articles; ≥2 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** At least moderately trained by authors' definition, often ~3 h/week.
- **Verified extraction:** No overall HIIT vs SIT TT difference. Long HIIT ≥4 min about 2% greater TT improvement than SIT. No VO2max difference.
- **Limitations:** Only six studies. Training status below the 7-12 h audience for many participants. Abstract only.

**Rosenblat MA, Lin E, da Costa BR, Thomas SG; 2021.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-021-01457-2). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-021-01457-2`. PMID: [33826121](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33826121/).
- **Design / duration:** Meta-analysis, 29 studies, 67 groups; ≥2 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** Mean VO2 52, range 32-70; inactive, active, and trained.
- **Verified extraction:** Trained athletes ~2% TT improvement vs up to 6% in less trained. HIIT TT change depended on work-bout duration, not intensity. Dose-response with sessions and total work for HIIT, not SIT.
- **Limitations:** Mixed sports and fitness. Abstract only.

<a id="int30"></a>
### INT30 — Effect of interval training on the factors influencing maximal oxygen consumption

**Rosenblat MA, Granata C, Thomas SG; 2022.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-021-01624-5). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-021-01624-5`. PMID: [35041180](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35041180/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; ≥1 week or ≥3 sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 32 studies, 369 participants, 49 women, mixed training status.
- **Verified extraction:** Only HIIT significantly increased maximal stroke volume (~14%) and cardiac output (~13%). Both HIIT and SIT increased citrate synthase. Capillary density increased after SIT in active individuals in the pool.
- **Limitations:** Not elite cyclists. Abstract only. Does not justify exclusive central vs peripheral format labels.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)

DOI: `10.1371/journal.pone.0073182`. PMID: [24066036](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24066036/).
- **Design / duration:** Meta-analysis 1965-2012; 6-13 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 334 sedentary or recreationally active adults <45 years.

<a id="int34"></a>
### INT34 — An examination and critique of current methods to determine exercise intensity

**Jamnick NA, Pettitt RW, Granata C, Pyne DB, Bishop DJ; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-020-01322-8). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-020-01322-8`. PMID: [32729096](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32729096/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** n/a.
- **Verified extraction:** Fixed percent of VO2max, HRmax, or peak work rate does not reliably place people in a given intensity domain. Critical power/speed better delineates heavy vs severe among common anchors. Acute responses do not guarantee chronic adaptations.

DOI: `10.2165/00007256-200737070-00002`. PMID: [17595153](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17595153/).

### INT46 — Effects of HIIT in cool and hot on temperate performance and physiological response in trained cyclists

**Boynton JR, Peiffer JJ, Abbiss CR; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/JSC.0000000000005013). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/JSC.0000000000005013`. PMID: [39977025](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39977025/).
- **Design / duration:** 4-week 5x4 min HIIT in 13 C vs 35 C; 8 sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 20 trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Time >90% VO2max 875±295 s cool vs 420±395 s hot. Temperate 20 km power +3.3±3.4% vs +7.3±6.3%, no between-group difference.
- **Limitations:** Heat confound. Not a format comparison. Abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)

### INT52 — Case studies in physiology: temporal changes in determinants of aerobic performance in an individual going from alpine skier to world junior champion time trial cyclist

**Rønnestad BR, Hansen J, Stensløkken L, Joyner MJ, Lundby C; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1152/japplphysiol.00798.2018). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Case study; 3 years of bike-specific training.
- **Population / baseline:** One male athlete.
- **Verified extraction:** VO2max rose from 74.6 to 96.7 mL/kg/min. Not an interval work-rate study. Recorded because it was a false candidate for the work-rate descriptor.
- **Limitations:** n=1. Irrelevant to format comparison except as a rejected match.

<a id="int53"></a>
### INT53 — Influence of immune and nutritional biomarkers on illness risk during interval training

**Hanstock HG, Govus AD, Stenqvist TB, Melin AK, Sylta Ø, Torstveit MK; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2018-0527). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2018-0527`. PMID: [31034260](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31034260/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomized 4-week comparison of three HIT prescriptions; primary outcomes were illness and biomarkers, not performance; 12 supervised HIT sessions over 4 weeks, plus ad libitum low-intensity training.
- **Population / baseline:** 27 well-trained male cyclists and triathletes enrolled; 25 analyzed, age 29.9±9.1, VO2peak 64.0±6.4 mL/kg/min, performance level 3-4.
- **Verified extraction:** LI: 4x8 min, 2 min recovery, 32 min work. SI1: 4x(12x40/20), 32 min work if 20 s rests excluded. SI2: 4x(8x40/20), 32 min only if 20 s rests counted as work. Isoeffort; recovery 50% of work power. Session HR about 86-87% HRmax and median RPE 17 did not differ. Cohort VO2peak increased moderately (d=0.65, 90% CI 0.16 to 1.13); magnitude not significantly different between groups. Group pre/post means are in a figure and were not extracted. No URI incidence, duration, or severity difference between groups.
- **Limitations:** Illness was the primary question. Small cells, n per arm not stated in text. Male only. Accepted manuscript, not the copyedited journal version. Does not show equivalence of formats.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://miun.diva-portal.org/smash/get/diva2%3A1313361/FULLTEXT01.pdf), [source 2](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31034260/)

<a id="int54"></a>
### INT54 — Optimizing interval training at power output associated with peak oxygen uptake in well-trained cyclists

**Rønnestad BR, Hansen J; 2016.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/JSC.0b013e3182a73e8a). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/JSC.0b013e3182a73e8a`. PMID: [23942167](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23942167/).
**Date note:** Verified journal issue is 2016; an earlier citation/DOI date was 2013. The full-text acute experiment is not a 2013 longitudinal trial.
- **Design / duration:** Acute randomized-order crossover to exhaustion; not a training study; Three sessions to exhaustion within 3 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 13 well-trained male cyclists, VO2peak 66.7±6.1 mL/kg/min, at least 6 h/week.
- **Verified extraction:** Work at MAP (375±41 W), recovery 50% MAP for half the work duration. Tmax 340±108 s. 30/15 time ≥90% VO2peak 678±270 s vs 397±156 s for 50% Tmax and 366±127 s for 80% Tmax (p<0.01). Time to exhaustion 1385±547 vs 827±283 vs 642±191 s. Peak RPE about 19 in all. In n=6, time ≥90% peak stroke volume longer in 30 s (787±447 vs 510±336 s). Authors: whether this produces larger training adaptations remained to be investigated.
- **Limitations:** Acute only. To exhaustion, not a fixed session. Male only. Journal issue 2016; DOI registered 2013. Stroke volume subset n=6.
- **Relevant chapters:** [VO₂-focused intervals](../topics/vo2-intervals.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://paulogentil.com/pdf/Optimizing%20Interval%20Training%20at%20Power%20Output%20Associated%20With%20Peak%20Oxygen%20Uptake%20in%20Well-Trained%20Cyclists.pdf), [source 2](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23942167/)

### DUR04 — The reliability of 4-minute and 20-minute time trials and their relationships to functional threshold power in trained cyclists

**MacInnis MJ; Thomas ACQ; Phillips SM; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2018-0100). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2018-0100`. PMID: [29809063](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29809063/).
- **Design / duration:** Reliability, one 60-min trial; Acute.
- **Population / baseline:** 8 trained men, VO2max 71±5.
- **Verified extraction:** 4-min TEM 8.1 W ICC 0.98; 20-min TEM 4.6 W ICC 0.99; CP from 4+20 TEM 6.5 W. Strong association with 60-min mean 309±26 W. Not a claim that 95% of 20 min equals 60 min.
- **Limitations:** n=8, one 60-min trial. Abstract.

### DUR05 — Is the FTP Test a Reliable, Reproducible and Functional Assessment Tool in Highly-Trained Athletes?

**McGrath E, Mahony N, Fleming N, Donne B; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.70252/RQOO7391). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.70252/RQOO7391`. PMID: [31839854](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31839854/).
- **Design / duration:** Repeated 20-min FTP and 60-min at 95% of 20-min power; Successive weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 19 highly trained athletes, Wattbike.
- **Verified extraction:** 20-min agreement bias +2 W, LoA +13 to -17 W. 17/19 completed 60 min at computed FTP in quasi-steady state; +5% destabilised lactate.

**Clark IE, Vanhatalo A, Thompson C, Joseph C, Black MI, Blackwell JR, Wylie LJ, Tan R, Bailey SJ, Wilkins BW, Kirby BS, Jones AM; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1152/japplphysiol.00207.2019). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Verified extraction:** EP unchanged at 40 min (268±39) and 80 min (260±40) vs control 260±37; after 2 h 236±47 W (-9%). CHO after 2 h 254±49 W. WEP lower by 80 min (14.7±2.9 kJ) and 2 h (13.8±2.7 vs 17.9±3.3); CHO did not preserve WEP (13.5±3.4 kJ).

### DUR35 — Substrate utilization and durability during prolonged intermittent exercise in elite road cyclists

**Ørtenblad N, Zachariassen M, Nielsen J, Gejl KD; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-024-05437-y). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-024-05437-y`. PMID: [38441690](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38441690/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute 4 h intermittent ride then 6-min TT; Acute.
- **Population / baseline:** 12 elite/professional cyclists, VO2peak 73.6 ml/kg/min.
- **Verified extraction:** 6-min power -10% [4, 15], peak power -6% [0, 6] despite 100 g/h CHO. Drop not predicted by fresh LT1, GE, MFO, VO2peak or in-ride fat oxidation.
- **Limitations:** Abstract only; CI formatting partly truncated.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/38441690)

<a id="dur36"></a>
### DUR36 — Is all work the same? Performance after accumulated work of differing intensities in male professional cyclists

**Mateo-March M, Leo P, Muriel X, Javaloyes A, Mujika I, Barranco-Gil D, Pallarés JG, Lucia A, Valenzuela PL; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jsams.2024.03.005). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1016/j.jsams.2024.03.005`. PMID: [38604818](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38604818/).
- **Design / duration:** Observational field, work above vs below CP; Season.
- **Population / baseline:** 17 male professional cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** CP fell 2.2%, 6.1% and 16.2% after 2.5, 5.0 and 7.5 kJ/kg above CP. Work-matched below-CP efforts did not similarly impair performance. MMP declines of about 2-4% reported after above-CP work.
- **Limitations:** Abstract truncated in places; observational; professionals only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/38604818)

<a id="dur37"></a>
### DUR37 — The Record Power Profile of Male Professional Cyclists: Fatigue Matters

**Mateo-March M, Valenzuela PL, Muriel X, Gandia-Soriano A, Zabala M, Lucia A, Pallares JG, Barranco-Gil D; 2022.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2021-0403). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2021-0403`. PMID: [35240578](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35240578/).
- **Design / duration:** Database of record MMP after 0, 15, 25, 35, 45 kJ/kg; Multi-season observational.
- **Population / baseline:** 112 male professionals, ProTeam and WorldTour, 2013-2021.
- **Verified extraction:** Fatigue changes the record power profile and separating categories. Percent declines were not in the retrieved abstract.
- **Limitations:** Abstract truncated before magnitudes; racing confounders.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)

<a id="dur38"></a>
### DUR38 — Reliability of the durability concept in professional cyclists: a field-based study

**Mateo-March M, Barranco-Gil D, Hernández-Belmonte A, Javaloyes A, Muriel X, Pallarés JG, Lucia A, Valenzuela PL; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1055/a-2555-8961). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1055/a-2555-8961`. PMID: [40373793](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40373793/).
- **Design / duration:** Repeatability of best MMP after 0-40 kJ/kg; Season.
- **Population / baseline:** 18 professional cyclists, 27±4 y.
- **Verified extraction:** Progressive MMP decline after higher accumulated work, particularly after 20 kJ/kg. Repeatability high for longer efforts, lowest for 10 s. Abstract reports ICC context as high but the numeric ICC was truncated.
- **Limitations:** Abstract only; does not prove trainability.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/40373793)

<a id="dur39"></a>
### DUR39 — Power Road-Derived Physical Performance Parameters in Junior, Under-23, and Professional Road Cycling Climbers

**Gallo G, Mateo-March M, Leo P, Campos-Donaire A, Gandia-Soriano A, Giorgi A, Faelli E, Ruggeri P, Codella R, Mujika I, Filipas L; 2022.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2021-0554). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2021-0554`. PMID: [35483700](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35483700/).
- **Design / duration:** Retrospective field comparison; 2016-19 seasons.
- **Population / baseline:** 53 male climbers: 15 junior, 21 U23, 17 professional.
- **Verified extraction:** High-ranked juniors separated by fresh record power. High-ranked U23 and pros separated by smaller percentage decline after 20-50 kJ/kg. Pros declined less than U23 high-ranked.
- **Limitations:** Abstract only; cross-sectional; selection and racing confounders.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)

<a id="dur40"></a>
### DUR40 — Is intensity the most important factor in determining the amount of prior work accumulated that affects cyclists' acute durability? A systematic review

**Sánchez-Jiménez JL, Salas-Montoro JA, Mateo-March M, Priego-Quesada JI, Zabala M, Pérez-Díaz JJ; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-025-05885-0). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-025-05885-0`. PMID: [40613880](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40613880/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review, 21 studies, search 18 Sep 2024; Acute studies.
- **Population / baseline:** Cyclists, mixed level.
- **Verified extraction:** Above-CP prior work: 10-20% power drops after 2.5-15 kJ/kg. Below-CP work often smaller drops at higher volumes. kJ alone insufficient. Practical 5x8 suggestion cites a fatigue protocol, not a training trial.
- **Limitations:** Protocol heterogeneity; about 30% of studies found by hand search; does not include 2025-2026 trials after the search date.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC12881052/fullTextXML)

### DUR41 — The Relationship between Physiological Characteristics and Durability in Male Professional Cyclists

**Spragg J, Leo P, Swart J; 2023.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/MSS.0000000000003024). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/MSS.0000000000003024`. PMID: [35977108](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35977108/).
- **Design / duration:** Fresh vs fatigued CP after 5x8 min at 105-110% CP; Acute.
- **Population / baseline:** 10 young professional men, age 19.2±0.8, VO2max 74.4±4.8, CP 5.6±0.6 W/kg.
- **Verified extraction:** Delta CP correlated with relative peak power r=0.891. Magnitude of the CP drop was not in the retrieved abstract.
- **Limitations:** Abstract truncated; very young professionals; n=10.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/35977108)

<a id="dur43"></a>
### DUR43 — Training Characteristics Related to (the Changes in) Durability in Semiprofessional Cyclists

**Voet JG, van Erp T, Viribay A, de Koning JJ, Lamberts RP; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2024-0321). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2024-0321`. PMID: [40054482](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40054482/).
- **Design / duration:** Observational 8-week coach-led preseason, fresh and fatigued 1- and 10-min TTs; 8 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 10 male semiprofessional cyclists, 22±4 y, 71.0±6.4 kg, LT2 347±30 W; training data n=9, 16.7±1.4 h/week.
- **Verified extraction:** Fatigued 10-min +25 W (18 to 33; 7.3%); fresh 10-min +21 W (8 to 34; 6.4%); fatigued 1-min +20 W (5 to 36; 3.6%); fresh 1-min +16 W (1 to 31; 2.5%). Preload 38.6±4.6 kJ/kg. No single load metric predicted improvement. Authors state no direct effect of a polarised pattern on fatigued TT power was shown.
- **Limitations:** Observational; small; one coach shared by half the sample; not 7-12 h amateurs.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://research.vu.nl/ws/files/423916107/ijspp-article-p644_Training_Characteristics_Related_to_the_Changes_in_Durability_in_Semiprofessional_Cyclists.pdf)

### DUR44 — Durability and Underlying Physiological Factors: How Do They Change Throughout a Cycling Season in Semiprofessional Cyclists?

**Voet JG, Lamberts RP, Viribay A, de Koning JJ, van Erp T; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2023-0543). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2023-0543`. PMID: [38871342](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38871342/).
- **Design / duration:** Three lab visits across a season, fresh and after ~38 kJ/kg; Season.
- **Population / baseline:** 16 male semiprofessional cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Abstract: 1-min TT more sensitive to fatigue than 10-min; durability described as improving more than fresh maximal power. Percent changes not in the retrieved abstract.
- **Limitations:** Abstract truncated; related to Voet 2025, not an independent method trial.

<a id="dur45"></a>
### DUR45 — Durability is improved by both low and high intensity endurance training

**Matomäki P, Heinonen OJ, Nummela A, Laukkanen J, Auvinen EP, Pirkola L, Kyröläinen H; 2023.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.3389/fphys.2023.1128111). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.3389/fphys.2023.1128111`. PMID: [36875044](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36875044/).
- **Design / duration:** 10-week LIT vs HIT cycling; 10 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 35 sedentary or recreationally active adults, 16 men 19 women.
- **Verified extraction:** Composite durability improved in both groups (LIT g=0.49, HIT g=0.62), interaction p=0.42. Test was 3 h at 48% of pre-training VO2max. VO2max rose only after HIT. Drift changes were modest.
- **Limitations:** Untrained; not transferable as an amateur prescription.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC9977827/fullTextXML)

### DUR46 — Strength training improves 5-min all-out performance following 185 min of cycling

**Ronnestad BR, Hansen EA, Raastad T; 2011.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1600-0838.2009.01035.x). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/j.1600-0838.2009.01035.x`. PMID: [19903319](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19903319/).
- **Design / duration:** Controlled intervention; same cohort as STR02; 12 weeks, same strength dose.
- **Population / baseline:** Same 20 well-trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** 5-min power after 185 min at 44% maximal aerobic power rose from 371+/-9 to 400+/-13 W in E+S; no change in E. Late-ride VO2, HR, lactate, and RPE fell more in E+S.
- **Limitations:** Not an independent replication. Steady submaximal preload, not a stochastic race.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md), [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/19903319)

### DUR47 — Effect of heavy strength training on thigh muscle cross-sectional area, performance determinants, and performance in well-trained cyclists

**Ronnestad BR, Hansen EA, Raastad T; 2010.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-009-1307-z). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-009-1307-z`. PMID: [19960350](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19960350/).
- **Design / duration:** Controlled training intervention, allocation method not stated as random in the abstract; 12 weeks; 4 lower-body exercises, 3x4-10RM, twice weekly.
- **Population / baseline:** 20 well-trained cyclists; E+S n=11 men; E n=9 (7 men, 2 women).
- **Verified extraction:** E+S increased thigh CSA, isometric force, Wingate peak power, power at 2 mmol/L, and 40-min mean power. VO2max rose similarly in both groups. Body mass did not rise with larger thigh. E 40-min power tended to improve (P=0.057).
- **Limitations:** Small; companion to the 185-min paper; abstract-only.

<a id="dur51"></a>
<a id="fuel06"></a>
### DUR51 — Graded Carbohydrate Ingestion up to 120 g·h−1 Attenuates the Reduction in Critical Power Following 3 h of Moderate-Intensity Exercise in a Dose-Dependent Manner

**Norte BR, Slinn MM, Johnson KO, Mahon E, Shepherd SO, Strauss JA, Louis JB; 2026.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.70326). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/sms.70326`. PMID: [42322010](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42322010/).
- **Design / duration:** Crossover, 3 h at 95% GET with 0, 60 or 120 g/h CHO, 3-min CP estimate; Acute; first publication 2026-06-01.
- **Population / baseline:** 16 endurance-trained cyclists/triathletes, VO2max 51.8±6.8.
- **Verified extraction:** After 24 h of 8 g/kg CHO plus a 2 g/kg meal, 3 h at 95% of estimated gas-exchange threshold (GET; first threshold, moderate domain, not FTP). Estimated CP: fresh 277±27 W; water 236±30; 60 g/h 257±28; 120 g/h 266±29. W' fell with no CHO-condition difference. 90 g/h was not tested. Six GI symptoms every 30 min (Figure 7): only 4/16 participants scored ≥4 and <8 in the 60 or 120 g/h conditions. Cumulative scores higher at 120 vs water (p=0.005; 18 [0-81] vs 6 [0-56]) but 120 vs 60 and 60 vs water not significant. Peak regurgitation, fullness and cramps higher at 120 vs water only; no 120-vs-60 difference.
- **Limitations:** 3-min estimate of CP; modest VO2max; n=16; 90 g/h not tested. Section heading says GI discomfort was not altered, but post hoc tests show 120 g/h worse than water on cumulative score and three peak symptoms. Not universal tolerance or intolerance. GET preload is not an FTP ride.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md), [Fueling and energy availability](../topics/fueling-energy-availability.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC13282445/fullTextXML), [source 2](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13282445/), [source 3](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.70326)
- **Citation aliases:** `DUR51`, `FUEL06`. See JSON for per-chapter extractions; aliases are not independent studies.

### DUR52 — Low carbohydrate availability reduces power output at the moderate-to-heavy transition, impairs efficiency, and increases median power frequency during cycling in females

**Main E, Yoon S, James SL, Mellor KM, Brick MJ, Leigh WB, Maunder E; 2026.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-026-06260-3). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-026-06260-3`. PMID: [42228164](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42228164/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomised counterbalanced crossover; Acute: glycogen depletion ~24 h prior, then incremental and 3-min all-out; CHO ≥9 vs ≤1 g/kg.
- **Population / baseline:** 9 well-trained female cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Original abstract: VT1 power 133 ± 24 W (low CHO) vs 152 ± 28 W (high), difference −19 ± 14 W; efficiency lower in low; no difference in lactate-threshold power or 3-min all-out end-test power. Wattage is original-abstract only and was not revised or confirmed by the correction (FUEL33).
- **Limitations:** Acute, not training. Wattage figures are abstract extracts. Correction fixes a duplicated figure and does not state a numeric results change.

**Impey SG, Hearris MA, Hammond KM, et al.; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-018-0867-7). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

<a id="dur58"></a>
### DUR58 — The Effect of 90 and 120 Min of Running on the Determinants of Endurance Performance in Well-Trained Male Marathon Runners

**Zanini M, Folland JP, Blagrove RC; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.70076). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/sms.70076`. PMID: [40375575](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40375575/).
- **Design / duration:** Acute, unfatigued vs after 90 and 120 min heavy-domain running; Acute.
- **Population / baseline:** 14 trained male marathoners, VO2max 63.1±5.8, marathon ~2:47.
- **Verified extraction:** VO2peak -3.1% after 90 min and -7.1% after 120 min. Oxygen cost +4.2% and +5.8%.
- **Limitations:** Running, not cycling; abstract only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Threshold, durability and long rides](../topics/threshold-durability-long-rides.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/40375575)

**Zanini M, Jones AM, Nybo L; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1152/japplphysiol.00671.2025). Access: **metadata**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1152/japplphysiol.00671.2025`. PMID: [41396096](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41396096/).
