<a id="str20"></a>
### STR20 — Strength training for middle- and long-distance performance: a meta-analysis

**Berryman N, Mujika I, Arvisais D, Roubeix M, Binet C, Bosquet L; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2017-0032). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2017-0032`. PMID: [28459360](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28459360/).
- **Design / duration:** Meta-analysis; Strength mesocycles of varying length.
- **Population / baseline:** Middle- and long-distance runners, cyclists, skiers, and swimmers; 28 studies.
- **Verified extraction:** Performance SMD 0.52 (0.33 to 0.70). Energy cost 0.65, maximal force 0.99, maximal power 0.50. Maximal-force training had larger effects than other intensities.
- **Limitations:** Not cycling-only.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/28459360)

<a id="str21"></a>
### STR21 — The effects of resistance training on road cycling performance among highly trained cyclists: a systematic review

**Yamamoto LM, Klau JF, Casa DJ, Kraemer WJ, Armstrong LE, Maresh CM; 2010.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/JSC.0b013e3181c86583). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/JSC.0b013e3181c86583`. PMID: [20072042](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20072042/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review of RCTs to September 2009; Five included trials.
- **Population / baseline:** Highly trained road cyclists, more than 7 h/week or 150 km/week.
- **Verified extraction:** Two of five trials that added resistance on top of existing endurance training found no performance gain. Three that replaced some endurance training with resistance training improved performance; two of those three used explosive-type work.
- **Limitations:** Small early evidence base. Later add-on trials complicate the replacement rule.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/20072042)

<a id="str22"></a>
### STR22 — Compatibility of concurrent aerobic and strength training for skeletal muscle size and function: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis

**Schumann M, Feuerbacher JF, Sunkeler M, Freitag N, Ronnestad BR, Doma K, Lundberg TR; 2022.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-021-01587-7). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-021-01587-7`. PMID: [34757594](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34757594/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; At least 4 weeks supervised.
- **Population / baseline:** Healthy adults; 43 studies; concurrent versus strength alone.
- **Verified extraction:** Maximal strength SMD -0.06 (-0.20 to 0.09); hypertrophy -0.01; explosive strength -0.28 (-0.48 to -0.08). Explosive-strength attenuation clearer in the same session than when separated by at least 3 h. Cycling versus running did not moderate the outcomes.
- **Limitations:** Outcome is strength, not cycling performance. Mostly non-cyclists.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/34757594)

<a id="str23"></a>
### STR23 — Concurrent training: a meta-analysis examining interference of aerobic and resistance exercises

**Wilson JM, Marin PJ, Rhea MR, Wilson SM, Loenneke JP, Anderson JC; 2012.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/JSC.0b013e31823a3e2d). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/JSC.0b013e31823a3e2d`. PMID: [22002517](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22002517/).
- **Design / duration:** Meta-analysis; Varied.
- **Population / baseline:** 21 studies, 422 effect sizes; mixed.
- **Verified extraction:** Running, but not cycling, associated with decrements in hypertrophy and strength. Endurance frequency and duration negatively related to hypertrophy, strength, and power.
- **Limitations:** Older than Schumann 2022. Not a 6 h versus 24 h comparison.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/22002517)

<a id="str24"></a>
### STR24 — Specific training effects of concurrent aerobic and strength exercises depend on recovery duration

**Robineau J, Babault N, Piscione J, Lacome M, Bigard AX; 2016.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/JSC.0000000000000798). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/JSC.0000000000000798`. PMID: [25546450](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25546450/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomised concurrent-training trial; 7 weeks; strength then aerobic; 0, 6, or 24 h gap.
- **Population / baseline:** 58 amateur rugby players.
- **Verified extraction:** Maximal strength gains lower at 0 h than at 6 h, 24 h, or strength-only. Some high-velocity strength and the VO2peak gain favoured 24 h over 0 h.
- **Limitations:** Rugby, not cyclists. Magnitude-based inference in the original reporting.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/25546450)

<a id="str25"></a>
### STR25 — High volume of endurance training impairs adaptations to 12 weeks of strength training in well-trained endurance athletes

**Ronnestad BR, Hansen EA, Raastad T; 2012.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-011-2112-z). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-011-2112-z`. PMID: [21833484](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21833484/).
- **Design / duration:** Controlled comparison of the same strength programme; 12 weeks, twice weekly.
- **Population / baseline:** Well-trained cyclists with no strength experience (n=11) versus non-endurance trainees (n=7).
- **Verified extraction:** Both groups gained 1RM, thigh CSA, and jump performance; relative gains and peak RFD were greater without the high endurance volume.
- **Limitations:** Control group is not cyclists. Shows blunted strength adaptation, not blunted cycling adaptation.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/21833484)

<a id="str26"></a>
### STR26 — The role of intra-session exercise sequence in the interference effect: a systematic review with meta-analysis

**Eddens L, van Someren K, Howatson G; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-017-0784-1). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-017-0784-1`. PMID: [28917030](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28917030/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; At least 5 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** Healthy adults; 10 studies.
- **Verified extraction:** Resistance-then-endurance favoured lower-body dynamic strength by about 6.91% (95% CI 1.96 to 11.87). No sequence effect on hypertrophy, static strength, or VO2max.
- **Limitations:** Not cyclists. Small sequence effect.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/28917030)

<a id="str27"></a>
### STR27 — The physiological effects of concurrent strength and endurance training sequence: a systematic review and meta-analysis

**Murlasits Z, Kneffel Z, Thalib L; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1080/02640414.2017.1364405). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1080/02640414.2017.1364405`. PMID: [28783467](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28783467/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; Chronic training.
- **Population / baseline:** Trials comparing strength-endurance versus endurance-strength order.
- **Verified extraction:** Lower-body 1RM higher by 3.96 kg (0.81 to 7.10) when strength preceded endurance. Aerobic capacity difference 0.39 ml/kg/min (-1.03 to 1.81).
- **Limitations:** Not cyclists.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/28783467)

<a id="str28"></a>
### STR28 — Maximizing adaptations in concurrent training: an umbrella review of meta-analyses

**Held S, Wolf L, Rappelt L, Bloch W, Donath L, Micke F, Geisler S, Isenmann E; 2026.** [Original source](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41762427/). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-026-02401-y`. PMID: [41762427](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41762427/).
- **Design / duration:** Umbrella review of meta-analyses; search to 28 February 2025; Epub 28 February 2026; journal June 2026.
- **Population / baseline:** 17 meta-analyses, 144 studies, 1492 healthy participants; recreational emphasis.
- **Verified extraction:** Concurrent training comparable to resistance training for strength, power, and hypertrophy, and better than endurance-only for strength (SMD 0.59). Versus resistance-only, aerobic capacity SMD 0.77. Highly trained data scarce. Retrieved abstract does not establish a 6 h rule.
- **Limitations:** Not cyclist-specific. Abstract wording on sequence is internally tense and was not over-interpreted.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41762427/)

<a id="str29"></a>
### STR29 — The impact of exercise-induced muscle damage on various cycling performance metrics: a systematic review and meta-analysis

**Devantier-Thomas B, Deakin GB, Crowther F, Schumann M, Doma K; 2024.** [Original source](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38241464/). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/JSC.0000000000004629`. PMID: [38241464](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38241464/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; Outcomes mainly 24-48 h after damage.
- **Population / baseline:** Studies of cycling after muscle-damaging exercise.
- **Verified extraction:** Submaximal VO2 unchanged; ventilation and RPE increased; sprint and incremental peak power reduced; time-trial and distance-trial performance not significantly changed. CK, DOMS, and force confirmed damage.
- **Limitations:** No significant time-trial change is not proof of no effect. Damage protocols are often unaccustomed.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38241464/)

<a id="str30"></a>
### STR30 — Training considerations for optimising endurance development: an alternate concurrent training perspective

**Doma K, Deakin GB, Schumann M, Bentley DJ; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-019-01072-2). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-019-01072-2`. PMID: [30847824](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30847824/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; Not a single trial.
- **Population / baseline:** Concurrent-training literature.
- **Verified extraction:** Resistance-induced muscle damage can impair subsequent neuromuscular function and endurance performance; intensity, mode, sequence, recovery, and contraction velocity modify that effect.
- **Limitations:** Narrative. No new pooled estimate.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30847824)

<a id="str31"></a>
### STR31 — The effectiveness of exercise interventions to prevent sports injuries: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials

**Lauersen JB, Bertelsen DM, Andersen LB; 2014.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1136/bjsports-2013-092538). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1136/bjsports-2013-092538`. PMID: [24100287](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24100287/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis of RCTs; Varied.
- **Population / baseline:** 25 trials, 26610 participants, 3464 injuries; not cycling-specific.
- **Verified extraction:** Strength training RR 0.315 (0.207-0.480). Stretching RR 0.963 (0.846-1.095). Proprioception RR 0.550.
- **Limitations:** Transfer to cycling overuse and crash injury is unproven.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)

<a id="str32"></a>
### STR32 — Strength training as superior, dose-dependent and safe prevention of acute and overuse sports injuries

**Lauersen JB, Andersen TE, Andersen LB; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1136/bjsports-2018-099078). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1136/bjsports-2018-099078`. PMID: [30131332](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30131332/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; Varied.
- **Population / baseline:** 6 studies, 7738 participants aged 12-40, 177 injuries; not cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Cluster-adjusted RR 0.338 (0.238-0.480). A 10% increase in strength-training volume associated with more than a 4 percentage-point risk reduction.
- **Limitations:** Not cycling. Dose finding is across heterogeneous programmes.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30131332)

**Wu H, Brooke-Wavell K, Fong DTP, Paquette MR, Blagrove RC; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-024-01993-7). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-024-01993-7`. PMID: [38261240](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38261240/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis of RCTs; Varied.
- **Population / baseline:** 9 articles, 1904 endurance runners.
- **Verified extraction:** No overall difference in injury risk (p=0.110) or rate (p=0.329). Post hoc supervised programmes looked more favourable; the retrieved abstract truncated that p value.
- **Limitations:** Runners, not cyclists. Post hoc.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/38261240)

DOI: `10.1177/0363546510376816`. PMID: [20847225](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20847225/).
- **Design / duration:** Descriptive epidemiology; interviews; Previous 12 months.
- **Population / baseline:** 109 of 116 professional road cyclists from 7 teams.
- **Verified extraction:** 94 overuse injuries; 45% low back, 23% knee. 58% had low-back pain; 36% anterior knee pain. Knee caused more time loss.
- **Limitations:** No prevention test. Professionals, not 7-12 h amateurs.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)

DOI: `10.1136/bmjsem-2024-002364`. PMID: [40692620](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40692620/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis of epidemiological studies; Search August 2024.
- **Population / baseline:** 44 studies across MTB, road, track, BMX, and para-cycling.
- **Verified extraction:** Upper-limb injuries, lacerations, abrasions, and fractures are common. Road cycling had a substantial share of head, neck, or face injuries. Not a prevention trial.

### STR36 — Comparing the effectiveness of the FIFA 11+ warm-up and conventional warm-up in enhancing cyclist performance and mitigating injury risk

**Panchal R, Rizvi MR, Sharma A, et al.; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-025-91005-z). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1038/s41598-025-91005-z`. PMID: [40108222](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40108222/).
- **Design / duration:** Pre-post comparative study; authors later call it quasi-experimental; 8 weeks; FIFA 11+ 20 min, 4 days/week, versus a longer conventional warm-up.
- **Population / baseline:** Male competitive cyclists aged 18-45. Methods: 79 enrolled, 69 included; abstract says 80. A priori calculation asked for 104..
- **Verified extraction:** Authors claim better core strength, balance, RPE, 3 km performance, and lower regional injury incidence with FIFA 11+. Injury metric is injuries per cyclist, not per exposure hour. Results narrative also refers to 3 km running. Limitations state no control group and unclear long-term injury effects.
- **Limitations:** Not an RCT. Internal inconsistencies. Do not use as proof of cycling injury prevention.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)

### STR49 — The acute effects of a cycling warm-up with and without foam rolling on biceps femoris stiffness and tissue homogeneity

**Earp JE, Joshi L, Mathisen C, Robinson D, Rehbein CO, Kraemer WJ, Penailillo L; 2026.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/JSC.0000000000005513). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/JSC.0000000000005513`. PMID: [42483891](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42483891/).
- **Design / duration:** Crossover acute study; Acute; first published 22 July 2026.
- **Population / baseline:** 21 healthy active subjects; not specified as cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Biceps femoris stiffness fell similarly after cycling with or without foam rolling. No cycling performance test. Authors say future work must test injury incidence.
- **Limitations:** Not a performance or injury trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/42483891)

<a id="str50"></a>
### STR50 — Acute prior heavy strength exercise bouts improve the 20-km cycling time trial performance

**Silva RA, Silva-Junior FL, Pinheiro FA, Souza PF, Boullosa DA, Pires FO; 2014.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/JSC.0000000000000442). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1519/JSC.0000000000000442`. PMID: [24584047](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24584047/).
- **Design / duration:** Crossover acute potentiation study; Acute; 4x5RM leg press 10 min before a 20 km time trial.
- **Population / baseline:** 11 trained cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Time to complete the 20 km trial fell 6.1%. Cycling economy index was greater. Pacing, lactate, and RPE did not differ in the retrieved abstract.
- **Limitations:** Acute, small, not a training adaptation. Can also fatigue other athletes.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/24584047)

<a id="str51"></a>
### STR51 — Post-activation potentiation versus post-activation performance enhancement in humans

**Blazevich AJ, Babault N; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.3389/fphys.2019.01359). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.3389/fphys.2019.01359`. PMID: [31736781](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31736781/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; Acute.
- **Population / baseline:** Human potentiation literature.
- **Verified extraction:** Twitch potentiation has a short half-life of about 28 s. Voluntary performance changes minutes later should not be equated with that mechanism.
- **Limitations:** Not a cycling trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31736781)

<a id="str52"></a>
### STR52 — Strength training among professional UCI road cyclists: practices, challenges, and rationales

**Vikestad V, Lyngstad IK, Dalen T; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0328195). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1371/journal.pone.0328195`. PMID: [40638631](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40638631/).
- **Design / duration:** Questionnaire and interviews; Cross-sectional practice survey.
- **Population / baseline:** 147 professional road cyclists (88 men, 59 women); 10 interviews.
- **Verified extraction:** Common pattern was two strength sessions per week off- and pre-season, dropping to one in the race season. More women than men reported in-season lifting. Barriers: travel, fatigue, racing. Rationales included performance, injury prevention, and bone health.
- **Limitations:** Practice, not efficacy. Injury rationale is not a trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/40638631)

<a id="str53"></a>
### STR53 — Strength training among male master cyclists-practices, challenges, and rationales

**Vikestad V, Dalen T; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.3390/jfmk9040232). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.3390/jfmk9040232`. PMID: [39584885](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39584885/).
- **Design / duration:** Questionnaire; Cross-sectional.
- **Population / baseline:** 555 male master cyclists aged 35 and older.
- **Verified extraction:** Strength frequency fell from off-season to race season. Barriers were fatigue and time. Bone health was a more common rationale in the older group.
- **Limitations:** Self-report. Not an outcome trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39584885)

<a id="str54"></a>
### STR54 — Strength training in the gym versus specific strength training on the bike in young off-road cyclists

**Vicari DSS, Filipas L, Giustino V, Drid P, Bianco A; 2026.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1093/bmb/ldaf024). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1093/bmb/ldaf024`. PMID: [41482961](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41482961/).
- **Design / duration:** Two-arm training comparison; 12 weeks; two sessions/week gym or on-bike strength plus endurance.
- **Population / baseline:** 19 young off-road cyclists; sex mix heterogeneous.
- **Verified extraction:** Gym arm increased 30-s Wingate mean power (P=0.002). On-bike arm did not (P=0.276). No significant jump-height change in either arm.
- **Limitations:** Small. Abstract truncated some maximal-power statistics. Not a time trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/41482961)

<a id="str55"></a>
### STR55 — Cycling-specific isometric resistance training improves peak power output in elite sprint cyclists

**Kordi M, Folland JP, Goodall S, et al.; 2020.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1111/sms.13742). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1111/sms.13742`. PMID: [32516483](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32516483/).
- **Design / duration:** Allocated training comparison; 6 weeks; isometric cycling-specific strength replaced usual compound lift.
- **Population / baseline:** 24 elite track sprint cyclists; EXP n=13, CON n=11.
- **Verified extraction:** Absolute PPO +46+/-62 W and relative PPO +0.8+/-0.7 W/kg in EXP. Authors state about 3-4% PPO improvement.
- **Limitations:** Track sprinters, not road amateurs. One p value stripped from the abstract.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/32516483)

<a id="str56"></a>
### STR56 — Strength training improves cycling efficiency in master endurance athletes

**Louis J, Hausswirth C, Easthope C, Brisswalter J; 2012.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-011-2013-1). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-011-2013-1`. PMID: [21638070](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21638070/).
- **Design / duration:** Pre-post training without an endurance-only control; 3 weeks; knee extensions 10x10 at 70% maximal repetition, 3 times/week.
- **Population / baseline:** 9 masters (51.5+/-5.5 years) and 8 young endurance athletes.
- **Verified extraction:** Masters MVC +17.8% versus +5.9% in the young. Delta efficiency rose significantly in masters and only tended to rise in the young. MVC and delta efficiency correlated in masters (r=0.79).
- **Limitations:** No endurance-only control. Short. Not heavy 4-10RM multi-joint work.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/21638070)

<a id="str57"></a>
### STR57 — Endurance and strength training effects on physiological and muscular parameters during prolonged cycling

**Hausswirth C, Argentin S, Bieuzen F, Le Meur Y, Couturier A, Brisswalter J; 2010.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jelekin.2009.04.008). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1016/j.jelekin.2009.04.008`. PMID: [19473854](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19473854/).
- **Design / duration:** Controlled training comparison; 5 weeks; strength 3 times/week.
- **Population / baseline:** 14 triathletes.
- **Verified extraction:** Combined training prevented the fall in freely chosen cadence over a 2-h ride. Exact physiological deltas were not recoverable.
- **Limitations:** Abstract markup incomplete for numbers. Triathletes. Short.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/19473854)

<a id="str58"></a>
### STR58 — Strength as a predictor of aerobic power in competitive cyclists: national team insights

**Javaloyes A, Sanchez-Munoz C, Salas-Montoro JA, Lillo-Bevia JR, Moya-Ramon M, Mateo-March M; 2026.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1055/a-2702-4879). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1055/a-2702-4879`. PMID: [40957605](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40957605/).
- **Design / duration:** Cross-sectional association study; 2-day test protocol; first published 16 September 2025.
- **Population / baseline:** 69 high-level and professional national-team cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** 1RM parallel squat associated with maximal aerobic power (r=0.73). Conclusion states strength was associated with maximal aerobic power but not time to exhaustion. Other coefficients were not cleanly recoverable.
- **Limitations:** Not causal. Not a training effect.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/40957605)

<a id="str59"></a>
### STR59 — Traditional versus velocity-based resistance training in competitive female cyclists: a randomized controlled trial

**Montalvo-Perez A, Alejo LB, Valenzuela PL, et al.; 2021.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.3389/fphys.2021.586113). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.3389/fphys.2021.586113`. PMID: [33716761](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33716761/).
- **Design / duration:** Randomised comparison of two lifting styles; no endurance-only arm; 6 weeks, twice weekly, plus usual endurance.
- **Population / baseline:** 17 competitive female cyclists; traditional n=8, velocity-based n=9.
- **Verified extraction:** Both improved strength. Velocity-based training was better for hip-thrust strength and power. No group-by-time difference for body composition or endurance performance.
- **Limitations:** No cycling-only control. Short.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Strength, warm-ups and mobility](../topics/strength-warmups-mobility.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://europepmc.org/article/MED/33716761)

<a id="str60"></a>
### STR60 — Effects of bicycle saddle height on knee injury risk and cycling performance

**Bini R, Hume PA, Croft JL; 2011.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.2165/11588740-000000000-00000). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.2165/11588740-000000000-00000`. PMID: [21615188](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21615188/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; Not a trial.
- **Population / baseline:** Saddle-height literature since 1960; 62 references.
- **Verified extraction:** Methods for setting saddle height disagree. Height can affect performance and knee-injury risk markers. Not a prevention RCT.
- **Limitations:** No single validated fit formula.

### STR61 — Prevention, evaluation, and rehabilitation of cycling-related injury

**Kotler DH, Babu AN, Robidoux G; 2016.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/JSR.0000000000000262). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1249/JSR.0000000000000262`. PMID: [27172085](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27172085/).
- **Design / duration:** Clinical commentary; Not a trial.
- **Population / baseline:** Recreational and competitive cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Recommends understanding traumatic and overuse mechanisms and early correction of strength and flexibility imbalances, technique, and bike fit.

### STR62 — The effectiveness of neuromuscular warm-up strategies, that require no additional equipment, for preventing lower limb injuries during sports participation: a systematic review

**Herman K, Barton C, Malliaras P, Morrissey D; 2012.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1186/1741-7015-10-75). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1186/1741-7015-10-75`. PMID: [22812375](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22812375/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review; Search to January 2012.
- **Population / baseline:** Sports participants in neuromuscular warm-up trials.

### STR63 — Acute effects of dynamic stretching on muscle flexibility and performance: an analysis of the current literature

**Opplert J, Babault N; 2018.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-017-0797-9). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-017-0797-9`. PMID: [29063454](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29063454/).
- **Design / duration:** Narrative review; Acute.
- **Population / baseline:** Dynamic-stretching studies.
- **Verified extraction:** Dynamic stretching generally increases range of motion and can have small positive effects on subsequent force, power, sprint, and jump when not excessive.

<a id="str64"></a>
### STR64 — Foam rolling and stretching do not provide superior acute flexibility and stiffness improvements compared to any other warm-up intervention: a systematic review with meta-analysis

**Warneke K, Ploschberger G, Lohmann LH, et al.; 2024.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jshs.2024.01.006). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1016/j.jshs.2024.01.006`. PMID: [38244921](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38244921/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review with meta-analysis; Acute.
- **Population / baseline:** 38 studies comparing stretching or foam rolling with other warm-up interventions.
- **Verified extraction:** Stretching and foam rolling did not provide superior acute flexibility and stiffness improvements versus other warm-ups that can raise muscle temperature.
- **Limitations:** Flexibility, not cycling power. Abstract used for the direction of the finding.

## Load and adaptation monitoring

<a id="mon01"></a>
### MON01 — Normalized Power, Intensity Factor and Training Stress Score

**Coggan AR; 2016.** [Original source](https://www.trainingpeaks.com/learn/articles/normalized-power-intensity-factor-training-stress/). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Vendor explainer; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Not a study.
- **Verified extraction:** Defines IF as NP/FTP. Describes NP as a model of delayed and curvilinear physiological responses, not a trial of the exponent or window.
- **Limitations:** Product documentation, updated 2026-05-11. Does not print the 30 s / fourth-power steps.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.trainingpeaks.com/learn/articles/normalized-power-intensity-factor-training-stress/)

<a id="mon02"></a>
### MON02 — Estimating Training Stress Score (TSS)

**Friel J; 2009.** [Original source](https://www.trainingpeaks.com/learn/articles/estimating-training-stress-score-tss/). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Vendor formula page; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Not a study.
- **Verified extraction:** States TSS = (sec x NP x IF)/(FTP x 3600) x 100 and that 1 h at FTP is 100 TSS by definition.
- **Limitations:** Updated 2026-04-13. Estimation table is coaching practice.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.trainingpeaks.com/learn/articles/estimating-training-stress-score-tss/)

<a id="mon03"></a>
### MON03 — What is the Performance Management Chart?

**TrainingPeaks; 2015.** [Original source](https://www.trainingpeaks.com/learn/articles/what-is-the-performance-management-chart/). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Vendor product page; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Not a study.
- **Verified extraction:** ATL is a 7-day exponentially weighted average of TSS labelled fatigue; CTL a 42-day average labelled fitness; TSB is yesterday's CTL minus yesterday's ATL. Form band slightly negative to +25 is a rule of thumb.
- **Limitations:** Updated 2026-05-16. Labels are product names, not measured fitness. Exact EWMA algebra not published on the page.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.trainingpeaks.com/learn/articles/what-is-the-performance-management-chart/)

<a id="mon04"></a>
### MON04 — Analysis of power output and training stress in cyclists: the development of the BikeScore algorithm

**Skiba PF; 2008.** [Original source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GoldenCheetah/GoldenCheetah/master/doc/user/bikescore.pdf). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Vendor/methods note; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Illustrative athlete files; small unpublished correlations.
- **Verified extraction:** Reconstructs Coggan NP as a 30 s moving average and a near-fourth-power lactate weighting. xPower steps: 25 s EWMA, raise to the 4th, mean, 4th root. Example NP 239 W vs xPower 236 W. Illustrative impulse-response fit used constants other than 42/7.
- **Limitations:** Not peer reviewed. 40 km correlations described as data not shown (n=5 and n=10).
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GoldenCheetah/GoldenCheetah/master/doc/user/bikescore.pdf)

<a id="mon05"></a>
### MON05 — Glossary of terms

**GoldenCheetah project; publication year unverified.** [Original source](https://github.com/GoldenCheetah/GoldenCheetah/wiki/UG_Glossary). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

**Date note:** Publication year was not verified for this software glossary; 2026 in the original extraction matches the access year, not a confirmed publication date.
- **Design / duration:** Software documentation; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Not a study.
- **Verified extraction:** Documents 30 s moving average for TSS/NP versus 25 s EWMA for xPower; CTL/ATL default time constants 42 and 7, claimed to relate to fitness and fatigue; TSB = yesterday CTL minus yesterday ATL; session-RPE = post-session RPE x minutes (citing Foster 2001); decoupling >5% heuristic qualified by heat and dehydration.
- **Limitations:** Implementation doc, not a validation trial.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://github.com/GoldenCheetah/GoldenCheetah/wiki/UG_Glossary)

### MON06 — Aerobic decoupling and heart rate drift explained

**Friel J; 2008.** [Original source](https://www.trainingpeaks.com/coach-blog/aerobic-endurance-and-decoupling/). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Coaching/vendor article; n/a.
- **Population / baseline:** Not a trial.
- **Verified extraction:** Defines decoupling as first-half versus second-half power:HR. Publishes <5%, 5-10%, >10% bands and states there is no hard research-based rule for how long coupling should last. Lists heat, dehydration, glycogen, and fatigue as causes of drift.
- **Limitations:** Updated 2026-06-03. Bands are coaching practice.

### MON09 — Comparison of heart rate and session rating of perceived exertion methods of defining exercise load in cyclists

**Rodriguez-Marroyo JA; Villa JG; Garcia-Lopez J; Foster C; 2012.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1519/jsc.0b013e31823a4233). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

<a id="mon16"></a>
### MON16 — Methods of monitoring training load and their relationships to changes in fitness and performance in competitive road cyclists

**Sanders D; Abt G; Hesselink MKC; Myers T; Akubat I; 2017.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2016-0454). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2016-0454`. PMID: [28095061](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28095061/).
- **Design / duration:** Observational preseason; 10 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 15 well-trained competitive road cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** All load methods correlated r=0.54-0.81 with changes in power at 2 and 4 mmol/L. Strongest fitness links: iTRIMP and TSS (r about 0.75-0.81). Strongest 8-min TT links: iTRIMP r=0.63 and Lucia TRIMP r=0.70.
- **Limitations:** Abstract. Not randomized. No CTL target.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=DOI:10.1123/ijspp.2016-0454&format=json&resultType=core)

<a id="mon17"></a>
### MON17 — A comparison of methods for quantifying training load: relationships between modelled and actual training responses

**Wallace LK; Slattery KM; Coutts AJ; 2014.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-013-2745-1). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-013-2745-1`. PMID: [24104194](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24104194/).
- **Design / duration:** Observational model fit; 15 weeks.
- **Population / baseline:** 7 trained runners, VO2max 51.7±4.5.
- **Verified extraction:** 1500 m improved 5.4±2.6%. Modelled vs actual performance r about 0.60-0.70 for rTSS, sRPE, and TRIMP. Time constants were fitted.
- **Limitations:** Runners, n=7. Abstract.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)

<a id="mon18"></a>
### MON18 — The influence of different training load quantification methods on the fitness-fatigue model

**Vermeire K et al.; 2021.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2020-0662). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2020-0662`. PMID: [33691278](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33691278/).
- **Design / duration:** Model comparison; 8 weeks training plus 3 weeks detraining; weekly 3 km TT.
- **Population / baseline:** 9 subjects, interval cycling.
- **Verified extraction:** Prediction error similar across Banister, Lucia, Edwards, TSS, sRPE, and kJ. Methods not interchangeable: the day of largest positive influence differed by up to 19.6 days.
- **Limitations:** Small sample. Abstract.

<a id="mon19"></a>
### MON19 — Relationship between various training-load measures in elite cyclists during training, road races, and time trials

**van Erp T; Foster C; de Koning JJ; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2017-0722). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2017-0722`. PMID: [30300025](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30300025/).
- **Design / duration:** Retrospective field; 4 years; 11655 sessions, 5445 with RPE.
- **Population / baseline:** 21 professional cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** kJ, sRPE, Lucia TRIMP, and TSS almost perfectly related in training and very large to almost perfect in races and TTs. TSS training-vs-race difference 120% versus 73%, 67%, and 68% for kJ, sRPE, and Lucia TRIMP.
- **Limitations:** Abstract. Elite men. Correlation is not interchangeability.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=EXT_ID:30300025&format=json&resultType=core)

**van Erp T; Hoozemans MPM; Foster C; de Koning JJ; 2019.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1123/ijspp.2018-0877). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1123/ijspp.2018-0877`. PMID: [30958053](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30958053/).
- **Design / duration:** Retrospective field; 4 years; 11716 sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 21 professional team cyclists.
- **Verified extraction:** Training IF 0.59±0.03 versus race IF 0.73±0.03. kJ predicted sRPE and Lucia TRIMP without an IF interaction; the kJ-TSS association depended on IF, consistent with quadratic IF weighting.
- **Limitations:** Abstract.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)

<a id="mon37"></a>
### MON37 — The 3-min all-out test in cycling: a systematic review and meta-analysis on its reliability and validity

**Quittmann OJ; Piehl MA; 2026.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-026-06356-w). Access: **full-text**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-026-06356-w`. PMID: [42479066](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42479066/).
- **Design / duration:** Systematic review and meta-analysis; Search 20 June 2025.
- **Population / baseline:** 20 studies, N=284, mostly small and male, 2006-2025.
- **Verified extraction:** EP ICC 0.89-0.99 and WEP ICC 0.76-0.98 if retested within 3 weeks with familiarization. EP overestimated CP (gz=0.408, PI 0.024 to 0.792). WEP underestimated W' (gz=-0.389, PI -0.954 to 0.176). Pooled EP vs CP: r=0.993, SEE 21 W, bias +11.7 W, LoA -37 to +61 W. WEP vs W' r=0.499, SEE 5.4 kJ.
- **Limitations:** Full text. Heterogeneous protocols. Prediction intervals wide.
- **Relevant chapters:** [Load and adaptation monitoring](../topics/load-monitoring.md)
- **Retrieval routes:** [source 1](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC13541879/fullTextXML)

<a id="mon48"></a>
### MON48 — Durability of the moderate-to-heavy intensity transition can be predicted using markers of physiological decoupling

**Rothschild JA et al.; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-025-05815-0). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s00421-025-05815-0`. PMID: [40402269](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40402269/).
- **Design / duration:** Pooled analysis of four laboratory studies; About 2.5 h moderate cycling.
- **Population / baseline:** 51 trained cyclists; 12 tested hourly to task failure.
- **Verified extraction:** HR and respiratory decoupling tracked durability of power at VT1. A model predicted concurrent VT1 power with mean absolute error about 7.2 W and R2 0.95 in that dataset.
- **Limitations:** Abstract. Not a validation of a 5% outdoor pass/fail. Model fit is not independent confirmation.

### MON49 — Predict carbohydrate and energy expenditure during endurance exercise using measures of training load

**Rothschild JA; Hofmeyr S; McLaren SJ; Maunder E; 2025.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40279-024-02131-z). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

DOI: `10.1007/s40279-024-02131-z`. PMID: [39487383](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39487383/).
- **Design / duration:** Laboratory prediction study; Four laboratory sessions.
- **Population / baseline:** 15 cyclists (5 women), VO2max 51.9±7.2, plus a validation sample.

**Manzi V; Iellamo F; Impellizzeri F; D'Ottavio S; Castagna C; 2009.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/mss.0b013e3181a6a959). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Population / baseline:** 8 recreational long-distance runners.

**Lucia A; Hoyos J; Santalla A; Earnest C; Chicharro JL; 2003.** [Original source](https://doi.org/10.1249/01.mss.0000064999.82036.b4). Access: **abstract**; checked 2026-10-02.

- **Design / duration:** Observational race comparison; Stage races.
- **Population / baseline:** 7 professional men, VO2max 74.6±2.2.
